# Judgments

Behaviour the kit decides where the official docs and types are silent (docs/SPEC.md). Each entry
names the code that holds it and why. A judgment is never counted as compatibility.

| Where                         | Judgment                                                                                          | Why                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/mods/load/guard-protection.ts` | The host classifies managed tool descriptors and additional protected rows through trusted identity callbacks on the original event; a failed lookup skips the user hook. A binding token prevents an older unload from disabling a newer guard. | The admin guide defines the protected artifacts but not private identity fields or overlapping host reloads. No private field shape is guessed. |
| `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts` | `next.origin` is a frozen copy rather than the embedding caller's mutable object. | Origin is readonly to a hook; the docs do not dictate ownership of the host's original object. |
| `src/mods/chain/registry.ts`, `src/mods/chain/only.ts`, `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts`, `src/mods/chain/trace.ts`, `tests/mods/chain/host-scope.test.ts` | A host that keeps one chain for many mods (a mod per project, loaded at different times) gives `ModPluginInfo.position`: mods of one tier run by it (lower first), then by when they registered, and a mod with none runs after those with one; a `caller` stands where its `position` puts it. `DispatchOptions.only` and `ModChain.hears(event, only)` take one name or a set of names (an unregistered name matches nothing; `hears` does not test matchers). `DispatchOptions.trace` is called once when the dispatch ends, also when it failed, with the mods that have a hook for the event (`chain`, once for each hook, in order) and a step for each function hook that answered (`passed` when it answered the value its `next` had already given, `returned` otherwise, with the event it received, its answer and its time) and for Claude Code's own behaviour (`engine`, tier `core`, the index after the last hook); a generator hook, a hook that failed and a core that streams or fails are no step. | events.md, "The order mods run in", gives the order by tier and by when mods load, one session at a time; it does not describe a host that holds several projects' mods in one chain, a way to ask whether anyone listens, or a record of what each hook did (a host's diagnostics). |
| `src/mods/api/settings-layers.ts` | The default settings adapter uses canonical repair and precedence, permits only supported plugin defaults, and retains original invalid-value floors. A host can provide its own merge port for application-specific fields. | Mod JSON layers do not carry the reader's invalid-value metadata; losing it would turn a fail-closed read into a grant. |
| `src/agents/preloaded-content.ts` | An exact command name wins over a bare-name alias; selected artifacts keep full parsed bodies without automatically running shell preprocessing. | Preload rules define selection and full content, not alias ties or when the host executes preprocessing. |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp-startup.ts` | Configured process variables are combined with the resolved host environment; host identity variables win on overlap. An unavailable executable has no process plan. | The docs require an installed executable and supplied variables, but do not prescribe a process-plan representation. |
| `src/format/manifest-paths.ts` | The `types` path follows the `./` prefix and containment rules of the component paths (the `.d.ts` extension is the field table's) | The table types it "Path" and the path rules name component keys; a `..` or absolute path would name a file outside the plugin |
| `src/format/manifest-schema.ts` | `options` with neither a `default` from the list nor `required: true` fails to load; the wording of the `options` messages | "Set `default` to one of the listed values, or set `required: true`" is read as a rule; "a plugin whose `options` break these rules fails to load" quotes no text |
| `src/loading/channels.ts` | A `channels` entry bound to a key that is not among the plugin's `mcpServers` is an error issue (the plugin still loads, without that channel); the wording | Channels says each entry is bound to one of the plugin's servers, not what a dangling one does |
| `src/loading/resolve/channel.ts` | A channel's options join the plugin's for its server's `env`, and win over a top-level option of the same key | The docs say channel values substitute into that server's `env`, not how a clash resolves |
| `src/loading/resolve/user-config-values.ts` | The value checked is the saved one else the `default`; an undeclared key is not checked; a text-typed option takes a string (an array when `multiple`), and the wording of every problem | The fields state what each accepts; the docs do not say what a loader does with a value that breaks them |
| `src/format/names.ts`         | The wording of the "not kebab-case" and "may not contain …" messages                              | The docs name the rules but quote no message for them                                     |
| `src/format/manifest.ts`      | The wording of "Unknown field", type-mismatch and missing-metadata messages                       | The docs say these are reported, not their text                                           |
| `src/format/paths.ts`         | The wording of "must start with ./" and "must be .md files"                                       | The docs state the rule; only the `..` message is quoted, and that one is used verbatim   |
| `src/format/manifest-schema.ts` | `author`, `dependencies` objects and `commands` entries are not strict (unknown keys dropped)   | "Unrecognized fields" lists the strict objects; these are not among them                  |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | A block with no closing `---` is not frontmatter (the whole file is body); the closing marker is the first later line that is only `---`; a leading byte order mark is ignored; an empty block is present with no fields | skills.md fixes only the opening line and the unparseable-YAML case |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | YAML is read with the 1.2 core schema (`yes`/`on` stay strings; a duplicate key does not parse) | The docs list the flag spellings but not the parser |
| `src/format/frontmatter-values.ts` | How a bad value is reported (the wording of each reason), that a number or boolean is read as text, that an enum matches exactly with no case folding, and that blank text is absent | The docs name the accepted spellings, not what happens to the rest |
| `src/format/frontmatter.ts` | A block that parses to a list or a single value is `not-a-table`: the file loads with no fields, like unparseable YAML; `line` and `column` count from the opening `---` as line 1 | The docs name only the unparseable case and send users to the parse error, not where it is |
| `src/format/frontmatter-values.ts` | `readInteger` takes a YAML number or a string of digits that is a safe integer, and reports a value outside `min`/`max` as dropped; `readList` has no default separator mode, each field picks one | The docs name the numeric limits and the list spellings per field, not how a bad one is handled |
| `src/loading/resolve/substitute.ts` | The grammar edges: a reference is `${name}` with no braces inside; a `${VAR:-default}` default ends at the first `}` and nests nothing; no escape; an unknown `${...}` stays as written; a `:-default` after a path variable is ignored; one pass, so a substituted value is never rescanned | mcp.md names `${VAR}` and `${VAR:-default}`, skills.md the single pass; neither gives the edges |
| `src/loading/resolve/substitute.ts` | The wording of the warnings (unset variable, option without a value, undeclared option), and that an unresolved reference stays as written | mcp.md says a missing variable is reported and the text kept, not the words; the docs say nothing for an option without a value |
| `src/loading/resolve/substitute.ts` | `SENSITIVE_PLACEHOLDER`: `[sensitive option KEY]` | manifest-reference says a sensitive value in skill and agent content "becomes a placeholder" and gives no text |
| `src/loading/resolve/user-config.ts` | `userConfigText`: numbers and booleans as `String(value)`, a list joined by commas; a key the manifest does not declare has no value; an option with no value and no default is not exported as `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_OPTION_<KEY>` | The docs show string options only and say "every option" is exported |
| `src/loading/resolve/variables.ts` | `Locations.pluginData` receives the sanitized `<id>` (`pluginDataId`), `pluginRoot` the plugin identifier as the host knows it | The docs define the `<id>` of the data folder; the kit owns that rule so a host only joins it |
| `src/loading/resolve/scope.ts` | The host says whether it is Windows (`SubstitutionScope.windows`); only the three path variables are written with forward slashes, never an option value or `${VAR}`; the process environment keeps native paths | "On Windows, most substituted paths use forward slashes"; the kit cannot tell the platform (L3) and the docs do not say which values are paths |
| `src/loading/resolve/surfaces.ts` | `${VAR}` expands only in MCP `command`, `args`, `env`, `url`, `headers` and in monitor `command`; elsewhere (hooks, LSP, `headersHelper`, content) it is left to the process | mcp.md and components.md (Monitors) give the fields; no other component documents `${VAR}` expansion |
| `src/loading/resolve/substitute.ts` | A field that refuses `${user_config.*}` (shell form, monitor, `headersHelper`) leaves the reference as written and reports nothing; the caller reports the documented error from the template (`referencesUserConfig`) | errors.md quotes one message per surface; the surface's resolver owns it, the substitution only guarantees a value is never put into a shell |
| `src/loading/resolve/surfaces.ts` | `mcp-remote` reads as empty exactly the names mcp.md writes (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NPM_TOKEN`); the MCP stream extends the set if the docs name more | The page lists covered names with "such as", so the full set is not published; a credential must never reach a remote server, so the named ones are covered now |
| `src/format/mcp-env.ts` | `displayHeaders` shows a header of a local, project or user server as written (a `${VAR}` reference by name); only the managed scope hides the values | managed-mcp.md gives only the managed rule (names without values); mcp.md says nothing of headers in `claude mcp get` for the other scopes |
| `src/format/mcp-endpoint.ts` | `mcpEndpointKey` is tested outside `spec(...)`: the id `mcpSettings.behavior.Scope hierarchy and precedence` stays unproven because the rest of the section (local over project over user over plugin over connector by name, the whole entry from the winner, the organization's server above all) is host selection the kit does not implement | The endpoint rules are only one of the section's rules; counting the section proven by them would overstate compatibility |
| `src/loading/resolve/data-id.ts` | A letter is `A-Z` or `a-z`: a non-ASCII letter (for example `\u00e9`) in a plugin identifier becomes `-` | manifest-reference says "letters, digits, `_`, or `-`" without saying whether letters are ASCII |
| `src/loading/resolve/exported-environment.ts` | A hook, MCP stdio or LSP process always gets `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA`, so the host is asked to create the data folder for each, referenced or not | The table says these processes receive the variable and the data folder is "created on first reference"; the docs do not say whether exporting counts |
| `src/loading/resolve/scope.ts` | `extra` variables (`CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_EFFORT`) are substituted on every surface, shell forms included, and win over `${VAR}` of the environment | skills.md describes them only for skill content; the kit has one substitution pass and the caller decides what goes into `extra` |
| `src/rules/parse.ts` | The wording of every rule issue except the two quoted ones (the malformed-rule message and the matches-no-tool warning): the mcp__ parentheses (settings files only), tool-glob specifier, unanchored allow glob and primary-field messages | The docs name the conditions and link error pages but quote no text for these |
| `src/rules/parse.ts` | The severity of a skipped rule is `error` (malformed, mcp__ with parentheses in a settings file, a glob with a specifier); an ignored or never-matching rule (primary-field parameter rule, unanchored allow glob) is a `warning` | permissions.md says "ignores it and emits a startup warning" for the primary field only; the rest only says "skipped" |
| `src/rules/parse.ts` | Surrounding whitespace of a settings entry is trimmed; `Tool()` reads as the bare tool name; a tool-name glob (`*` in the tool name) with a specifier is skipped | The docs give the grammar `Tool` or `Tool(content)` and the bare `Bash(*)`, nothing on blanks, empty parentheses or a glob with a specifier |
| `src/rules/specifier-formats.ts` | `Tool(*)` reads as the bare tool name for the command format (Bash and Monitor), and for no other tool | Only `Bash(*)` is documented as equivalent to `Bash`; Monitor shares Bash's rule format in the tools-reference table |
| `src/rules/param.ts`, `src/rules/specifier-formats.ts` | How `name:value` is told from a command, path or skill pattern: a parameter name is an identifier of two letters or more or a `-x` flag; for Bash, Monitor and PowerShell `name:value` is a parameter rule only when `name` is a field of the tool's input, and `name:*` stays the `:*` suffix; `domain:` is a domain pattern for WebFetch | permissions.md fixes the `Tool(param:value)` form and the `:*` suffix but not how they are told apart; `git:* push` and a Windows `C:\x` path must stay patterns |
| `src/rules/param.ts` | A number or boolean input compares as its text (`run_in_background:true` matches the boolean `true`, `timeout:5000` the number 5000); an object, array, null or missing field never matches; only own fields of the input count | The docs say "the literal input Claude sends" and "scalar parameter", not how a non-string scalar is written; `Bash(run_in_background:true)` is the documented example |
| `src/rules/param.ts` | Whitespace is trimmed around the colon and at both ends of the name and the value | The docs say "Whitespace around the colon is ignored", not about the ends |
| `src/rules/specifier-formats.ts`, `src/rules/evaluate.ts` | A text or domain specifier on a tool the "Rule format" table does not list goes to the `bare` matcher; until its sub-package replaces it the kit's matcher of every format fails closed: an allow rule matches nothing, a deny or ask rule matches every call of its tool | The table says unlisted tools "accept only the bare tool name", not what a specifier on one does; a matcher that skips a deny rule would let the call through unchecked, so the placeholder errs toward denying and asking |
| `src/rules/tool-pattern.ts` | A tool-name glob knows only `*` (any characters, case-sensitive, whole name); `?` and `[...]` are literal | permissions.md names `*` in every example of "Tool name wildcards" and says nothing of other glob characters |
| `src/rules/issues.ts` | The matches-no-tool warning uses the longer stderr form quoted in errors.md, with the rule as it was written (`PermissionRule.text`, trimmed), at path `permissions.<behavior>` | errors.md quotes both forms (the startup row and the stderr line); the kit has one line to give |
| `src/protocol/tool-names.ts` | `DOCUMENTED_TOOLS` holds the 46 names of the tools-reference table (the design said 53): the 45 that are not removed plus the nine SDK-only names are the SDK's 54 `BUILTIN_TOOL_NAMES` | The table is the source; the count in the design was wrong |
| `src/rules/sources.ts` | The policy an embedding host passes (`managedSettings`) is `embedder-managed`, read as settings content: an `mcp__` rule with parentheses is skipped there like in a file; the command line and the session are the only sources that read it as a parameter rule | permissions.md says the skip is for "a settings file" and names `--disallowedTools` as the way around it; it does not place the SDK option |
| `src/rules/parse.ts` | An allow rule `mcp__server__tool(x:y)` from the command line or the session parses as a text specifier and approves nothing | The docs name only "a deny rule with `--disallowedTools`" for an MCP parameter, and an allow rule never matches by parameter |
| `src/rules/param.ts`, `src/rules/types.ts` | The names a skill goes by come from the host (`PreparedCall.skillNames`); only a deny rule matches `Skill(skill:<name>)` under any of them, an ask rule matches the literal input | permissions.md says "deny rule" and skills.md owns the names (alias, display name, nested and synced forms); the kit holds no skill catalog |
| `src/rules/param.ts` | A `Monitor(command:x)` rule is an ordinary parameter rule: the primary fields are the per-tool list of permissions.md, which names Bash and PowerShell but not Monitor | The docs are silent on Monitor; the kit does not extend a list the docs give |
| `src/rules/param.ts`, `src/rules/specifier-formats.ts` | PowerShell has no SDK input type, so its primary field is `command` as the docs say and its parameter names are Bash's input fields | The SDK types `BashInput` and `MonitorInput` only; hooks.md gives PowerShell's `tool_input` no field list |
| `src/rules/param.ts` | A rule that names two parameters (`Agent(model:opus, isolation:worktree)`) reads the text after the first colon as one value, so it matches neither call | The docs say to write two rules "rather than combining them", not what a combined rule does |
| `src/rules/bash/split.ts`, `src/rules/bash/keywords.ts` | The command splitter is the kit's own quote-aware scanner: a command is split at the documented separators outside single and double quotes and after a backslash; a `&` that belongs to a redirect (`2>&1`, `<&3`, `&>`) is not a separator; commands inside `$()`, backticks, `( )`, process substitution and, for PowerShell, `{ }` are subcommands too; `${ }` and `$(( ))` are text except for what is substituted inside; `do`, `then`, `else`, `if`, `elif`, `while`, `until`, `!`, `{`, `case x in` and a `a)` label are keywords before a command, `done`, `fi`, `esac`, `}` and `for`/`select` headers are not commands; comments are not recognised; a here-document with a quoted delimiter has an inert body, one with an unquoted delimiter is read as commands, and the `<<` of a here-string (`<<< 'x'`) opens no here-document; nesting past 32 levels is unparseable | permissions.md names the separators and the nested forms but not an algorithm; every choice errs toward more subcommands, which only prompts more for an allow rule and denies more for a deny rule |
| `src/rules/bash/split.ts`, `src/rules/bash/shell.ts` | The only unparseable commands are those with nothing after `&&` or `||`, an unclosed quote, substitution or bracket, and nesting past the depth limit; the 10,000-character limit is a read-only-set rule and is not applied to rule matching. An unparseable command never matches an allow rule (not even by its whole text); a deny or ask rule tries the subcommands found so far and the whole text | permissions.md gives `npm test &&` as the example and says the command is not split "for allow-rule matching"; it says nothing of deny and ask, which must not let a call through unchecked |
| `src/rules/bash/wrappers.ts` | A rule is tried against a subcommand as written and after each wrapper or assignment is stripped in turn, not only the fully stripped form; the options of each wrapper (`timeout -s SIG -k DUR DURATION`, `nice -n N` or `-N`, `stdbuf -oL`, `time -p`, `command -p`) are the kit's reading of their manuals, `command -v` and `-V` are not stripped, and a wrapper with no command after it is left alone | The docs say "also matches" and "strips a fixed set" but not the options each takes, and a rule that names the wrapper (`Bash(timeout *)`) must keep working |
| `src/rules/bash/wrappers.ts` | The known-safe environment variables are `NODE_ENV`, `LANG` and `NO_COLOR`, plus `MatchContext.safeEnvVars`; a leading assignment is `NAME=value` with the name an identifier | The docs say "certain known-safe environment variables" and name those three as examples |
| `src/rules/bash/exec-wrappers.ts` | Besides `-exec`, `-delete`, `-rm`, `-fprint` and `-files0-from`, `find` with `-execdir`, `-ok`, `-okdir`, `-fprint0`, `-fprintf` or `-fls` needs an exact rule; wrappers and assignments before the command are seen through; the guard applies to allow rules that hold a `*` (or the `:*` suffix) and never to a deny or ask rule | The docs say "an action that runs commands, deletes files, or writes files, such as" four names; the others are find's actions of the same classes |
| `src/rules/bash/powershell.ts`, `src/rules/bash/dialect.ts` | PowerShell commands are split by the same scanner with `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|` and newline as separators, the backtick as the escape character, `{ }` as a block that holds commands, and `&` as the call operator; the alias table is `gci`, `ls` and `dir` for `Get-ChildItem`, applied to the first word of each subcommand and of the rule, so a rule written with an alias matches the cmdlet and the other aliases; no wrappers or assignments are stripped | The docs describe a PowerShell AST the kit does not have and name three aliases; resolving both sides keeps `PowerShell(ls *)` and `PowerShell(Get-ChildItem *)` the same rule |
| `src/rules/bash/if-eval.ts` | `evaluateIf` reads the `if` text as a deny rule from the command line (any subcommand matches; `name:value` is a parameter rule), runs the hook for a rule it cannot read (with the rule's issues), for a command with no string `command`, and for an unparseable command; "specifies more than the command name" is a pattern whose text before the trailing ` *` has more than one word, and `$VAR` is `$` followed by a letter, `_` or `{`, found by the splitter's quote-aware scan (outside single quotes, not escaped, an apostrophe inside double quotes opens nothing); a `Bash(...)` rule also answers a Monitor call, a `Monitor(...)` rule never a Bash call (hooks.md, "Match Monitor commands"); an issue about the `if` text is reported at path `hooks.if` (the hooks engine may refine it) with a note that the filter does not apply and the hook runs for every call, the rule being read as a deny rule only to reuse the permission-rule parser | hooks.md says the filter is best effort and runs the hook when it "can't determine" the commands, and gives one example of a pattern that says more than the name |
| `src/rules/bash/wildcard-warning.ts` | The wildcard-before-subcommand warning applies to a `Bash` allow rule whose first `*` word follows only options after the program and is followed by a literal word (`git * main`, `git -C * status *`), never to `Bash(git log * main)`, a rule with a wildcard program, or a deny or ask rule; its text is the stderr form of errors.md without the program-specific sentence and the settings path | errors.md describes "a `*` before a later word that determines which command it is" without a rule for where the subcommand is, and its quoted text names the settings file, which the kit does not know |
| `src/rules/path/depth.ts` | "A relative pattern with a single directory segment" is a pattern with one literal directory name, one slash and something below it (`src/**`, `src/*.ts`, `src/index.ts`), including a `**` between the directory and the rest (`src/**/*.ts`); a deny or ask rule of that shape matches at any depth under the cwd, an allow rule only at the anchor. A pattern starting with `/`, `**` or `!`, or holding two directories, is another shape | The docs show `src/**` and `secrets/**` only; widening the other one-directory shapes errs towards blocking for deny and ask and changes nothing for allow |
| `src/rules/path/anchor.ts` | `./path` is read as `path`: the same anchor, but a bare name or glob with no slash (`./.env`, `./*.env`) is anchored at the cwd and does not match at any depth, as `Read(.env)` does; a `../` prefix moves the anchor to the parent directory of the cwd, once per `../` | The docs list `path` and `./path` as one pattern type, say `Read(./.env)` matches "the `.env` file in the current directory" and `Read(.env)` any `.env` at or under it, and say nothing of `../` |
| `src/rules/path/anchor.ts` | A `/path` rule from the managed tiers (`managed`, `embedder-managed`) anchors at the primary working directory, like CLI and session rules; a `--settings` file given relatively resolves against the primary working directory | The source table lists five sources and no managed tier |
| `src/rules/path/glob.ts` | Matching is case-sensitive for a POSIX host and case-insensitive for a Windows host (`PathContext.platform`); a pattern is usable unless it is blank (`./` alone), a `#` comment or `/` alone, and an unusable deny or ask pattern guards the one path it spells (anchor root plus the text; `/` alone spells the anchor itself, not the filesystem root); `..hidden` is a name, only `..` and `../x` are parent climbs | The docs call an unusable pattern one that is "not usable as a gitignore pattern" without a list, and are silent on case |
| `src/rules/path/glob.ts`, `src/rules/path/tools.ts` | A Grep or Glob call is a search of a directory: a pattern covers it when it covers the path itself or the files below it (`Read(docs/**)` covers a search of `docs`), and a search with no `path` searches the cwd, which no pattern under the cwd covers | permissions.md says an ask rule prompts "when Claude calls Grep or Glob on a matching path, but not when a search of a working directory above it returns matching files"; how a directory matches `docs/**` is not stated, gitignore itself does not match `docs` |
| `src/rules/path/file-rules.ts` | `Write(*)`, `Glob(*)`, `Grep(*)`, `NotebookEdit(*)` and the legacy `MultiEdit(*)` cover every use of the tool; any other path on these five never matches. MultiEdit carries its path in `file_path` like Edit (the SDK has no type for it): an Edit rule covers it for allow, ask and deny, and a Read deny rule blocks it like Edit. An LSP path rule is read as unreadable: a deny or ask rule covers every LSP call, an allow rule none; a Read deny or ask rule reaches LSP the same way (no `!` carve-out reopens it), a Read allow rule does not. A Read ask rule does not reach Edit and Write, and an Edit deny or ask rule does not reach Read | The docs say these rules are accepted but never match "a path other than `*`" and to write the bare name for every use; MultiEdit is listed as a legacy tool "Edit rules cover" but has no input type; LSP is in the Read row of the format table ("Read, Grep, Glob, LSP") with no path field named, so the kit errs towards blocking; the Read-deny and Edit-allow implications are stated for those behaviours only |
| `src/rules/path/negation.ts` | A `!` rule carves out of the rules listed before it in the same list: the same source (a `--settings` file by its path), behaviour and tool name; only deny and ask rules carve, an allow rule starting with `!` approves nothing, and a `!` rule never covers a path itself | The docs describe "one settings file's `deny` list" and the same-source limit; the ask list and the tool boundary follow from "the rules listed before it" |
| `src/ports/path-resolver.ts`, `src/rules/path/symlink.ts` | `PathResolver` takes and returns the kit's POSIX form (on Windows `/c/...`); `prepareCall` resolves the nearest existing parent of a path that does not exist and appends the rest, records the target in `resolvedPaths` only when it differs, and returns a resolver's `PortFailure` instead of a call, so the host refuses the operation; a rule's real directory comes from `resolveRuleDirectories` (posix hosts, deny and ask rules written with `//`, `~/` or `/`, the directory before the first wildcard) | The docs say what is checked and that an unresolvable path is refused, not how a host resolves it or how a rule's directory is found |
| `src/rules/path/cd.ts` | `Cd(<pattern>)` compiles to a whole-path expression: `*` is within one segment, `**` between segments is zero or more segments, a trailing `/**` also matches its root; a relative pattern is under the cwd | The table gives four examples; `**` in the middle and the empty match follow the same reading |
| `src/rules/path/network.ts` | `readsNetworkPath` covers Read, Grep and Glob (the tools that read files), by the named path or any resolved one; a Windows path is a network path when it starts with `\\` or `//`, a POSIX path when it is `/net` or under it | The docs name "Read, Grep, and Glob" as examples ("such as") and one UNC and one `/net` spelling |
| `src/rules/target/domain.ts` | A `*` that is not a leading `*.` or the whole pattern matches any run of characters within one label (`[^.]*`), so `docs-*.example.com` and `*.example.*` work and no wildcard crosses a dot | permissions.md says the wildcard "matches only the text between two dots" and gives `example.*`; a part of a label is not addressed |
| `src/rules/target/domain.ts` | A URL that does not parse, or has no host (`example.com`, `mailto:a@b.c`, an empty text), has no hostname: no `WebFetch(domain:...)` rule matches it, bare `*` included; a `WebFetch(...)` specifier without `domain:` matches nothing | The docs define domain rules over "the hostname of the requested URL" and give WebFetch no other specifier form; the tool cannot fetch a URL without a host |
| `src/rules/target/domain.ts` | A rule pattern with non-ASCII labels is converted label by label to the punycode `URL.hostname` gives (`bücher.de` is `xn--bcher-kva.de`) before matching, so either spelling of the rule meets either spelling of the URL; a label holding `*` or URL syntax stays as written, and `sandboxDomainEntry` keeps the domain as written | permissions.md is silent on internationalised names; the URL parser always spells the host in punycode, so an unconverted rule could never match it |
| `src/rules/target/domain.ts`, `src/rules/evaluate.ts` | An artifact read is a call of `Artifact` with `action: read` and a `url` (the SDK's `ArtifactInput`); a deny or ask `WebFetch(domain:...)` rule covers it when the pattern matches the URL's host or the content host `*.frame.claudeusercontent.com` (probed with `artifact.frame.claudeusercontent.com`); an allow rule never pre-approves it, and a bare `WebFetch` rule never applies because its tool name is not `Artifact`. A `WebFetch` call for an artifact link is an ordinary fetch | permissions.md says a `domain:` rule covering `claude.ai` or `*.claudeusercontent.com` applies to artifact reads, a bare rule does not, and an `Artifact` rule does; it names no call shape, no content host name and nothing about allow rules |
| `src/rules/target/domain.ts` | `sandboxDomainEntry` returns the domain lowercased with the trailing dot stripped, only for allow (allowed list) and deny (denied list) rules, and nothing for a pattern with a `*` outside a leading `*.` or a bare `*`; an ask rule adds nothing | permissions.md and sandboxing.md name allow and deny rules and the two wildcard forms the sandbox honors; the normalisation is the hostname rule applied to the entry |
| `src/rules/target/mcp.ts` | The call carries no server name, so as an allow rule `mcp__<server>` covers `mcp__<server>__<tool>` only when `<server>` holds no `__` and does not end in `_` and `<tool>` holds no `__`; deny and ask rules also cover any name that starts with the rule's name and `__` | permissions.md gives the outcome for a server named `reports__v2` (allow does not match, deny and ask do) and says to write the wildcard form for a name ending in `_`; the kit has only the tool name to tell the servers apart |
| `src/rules/target/mcp.ts` | `mcpToolName` normalises the server name and leaves the tool name as the server declares it; a bare `Bash` or `WebFetch` deny rule (no specifier) covers the Cowork tools `mcp__workspace__bash` and `mcp__workspace__web_fetch`, an ask rule does not | mcp.md says the tool name is the declared name; permissions.md names deny rules that "name the whole Bash or WebFetch tool" and no other behaviour |
| `src/rules/target/agent.ts` | `Agent(Name)` compares the name with the call's `subagent_type` exactly (case-sensitive, `*` literal); a call without `subagent_type` counts as `general-purpose` for a deny or ask rule and matches no name for an allow rule; a fork is the call whose `subagent_type` is `fork`, so `Agent(fork)` names it | The docs say the rule "matches the subagent's name field" and give no wildcard or case rule; an omitted `subagent_type` falls back to `general-purpose` only in a session that has it, which the kit cannot see, so a deny or ask rule covers the fallback (a session without it fails the call, so the cover costs nothing) and an allow rule approves nothing it cannot see named; sub-agents.md says `Agent(fork)` stops forks without saying how the call spells it, and the SDK's `AgentInput` documents `subagent_type: "fork"` |
| `src/rules/specifier-formats.ts` | A `name:value` specifier of an Agent rule is a parameter rule only when `name` is a field of the SDK's `AgentInput`, and of a Skill rule only when it is `skill`; any other text is the name pattern | `Skill(deploy:deploy)` and a plugin-qualified `Agent(acme:reviewer)` are names (skills.md), and would otherwise silently match nothing as a parameter on a field the input never has |
| `src/rules/target/skill.ts` | `Skill(name)` and `Skill(name *)` read only the call's `skill`, never its `args`: the exact form covers the skill called with any arguments, `name *` covers `name` and `name:...`; a deny rule also tries every name in `PreparedCall.skillNames`, an allow or ask rule only `skill` and the skill's own name (`skillNames[0]`, the command): an ask rule reads what an allow rule does, the docs naming only allow and deny | skills.md says `name *` is a prefix match with any arguments and that `anthropic *` does not reach `anthropic-skills:pdf`; reading the arguments into an exact rule would let `deploy prod` pass `Skill(deploy)` |
| `src/rules/target/skill.ts` | A `*` in a `Skill` pattern other than the trailing ` *` is a literal character, so `Skill(deploy*)` and `Skill(*)` match only a skill named `deploy*` or `*` | skills.md defines only `Skill(name)` and `Skill(name *)` and says `anthropic *` does not reach `anthropic-skills:pdf`; no other wildcard is described |
| `src/rules/hook-matcher.ts` | A matcher other than match-all never selects an event that has no value; an invalid regular expression matches nothing instead of throwing; empty alternatives (`Edit|`) and an all-space matcher select nothing | The docs fix the three evaluation paths, not these edges |
| `src/format/hooks-settings.ts` | Any error leaves `hooks` empty (the whole file does not load); the issue message is the reason alone, the caller adds `Failed to load hooks from <path>:` | The docs say a file that fails the schema does not load, and the kit is not told the path |
| `src/format/hooks-settings.ts` | An event name that is not a hook event is warned about, and kept by `settings.json` hooks but not by a plugin's (oracle 2.1.319, below); a matcher that does not compile is kept with a warning; an unknown key of a matcher group is dropped with a warning; other top-level keys of a hooks file (`modules`) are ignored without an issue, and a file with `modules` and no `hooks` holds no hooks and is valid; a matcher that does not compile is not warned about on an event with no matcher support (it is ignored there) | The docs name no schema failure for these; the official type indexes events by any string |
| `src/format/hooks-handlers.ts` | A field that another handler type has, or that no type has, is dropped with a warning; `timeout` must be a positive finite number (the message says so for any bad value); an empty `command` is an error; the wording of every message | The docs list the fields per type and say nothing of strays, bounds or wording |
| `src/format/hooks-handlers.ts` | `continueOnBlock` is read on prompt hooks | It is in the official type but not in the docs' field table |

| `src/hooks/select/lock-gates.ts` | Under `allowManagedHooksOnly` only managed settings, SDK (`session`) and force-enabled plugin hooks run (matched on the full `plugin@marketplace` id in `session.forceEnabledPlugins`), a skill's frontmatter hooks run only when the skill is managed or from such a plugin, an agent's never. Under the plugin-only lock settings, skill and agent hooks run unless their scope is user, project or local and they are neither managed nor a plugin's; a source with no scope (a bundled skill, a `--settings` file) is not the user's own. The host leaves skills-directory plugins out of the registry | settings-reference.md lists what runs under each setting and does not say how a skill's frontmatter hooks or a `--settings` file fare under `allowManagedHooksOnly`, nor how a host marks a bundled skill; the skills-directory plugin exception needs a source kind the registry does not carry |
| `src/hooks/select/matcher-targets.ts` | A `FileChanged` matcher is read in the narrow grammar for the filter too; the value is the basename of `file_path`, split on `/` or `\` | the docs say the narrow set applies to the two events and that the filter uses "the standard matcher rules" against the basename, which disagree when the matcher holds a hyphen, comma or space |
| `src/hooks/select/watch-list.ts` | An empty segment of a `FileChanged` matcher (`.env\|`, or the empty string) registers no watch; the list holds each name once, in matcher order; a handler that never runs (gated off, or of a type `FileChanged` does not support) registers no watch | hooks.md registers "each segment" literally and says an omitted matcher adds nothing; it does not say whether a handler that cannot run still watches |
| `src/hooks/select/once.ts` | A hook from a plugin's hooks file or registered by the session never honours `once`, as a settings or agent hook does not | hooks.md says `once` is "Only honored for hooks declared in skill frontmatter; ignored in settings files and agent frontmatter" and names no other source |

| `src/hooks/input/common.ts` | `effort` is sent on `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure`, `PostToolBatch`, `PermissionRequest`, `PermissionDenied`, `Stop` and `SubagentStop`, when the session has an effort | hooks.md names four events and "other events with a tool-use context"; the SDK says "absent for session-lifecycle hooks"; the other events that name a tool call are counted as tool-use context |
| `src/hooks/input/common.ts` | An empty `prompt_id`, `scratchpad_dir`, `agent_id` or `agent_type` of the session counts as none and the field is absent | The docs call these fields absent, not empty, and a host with no value may pass an empty text |
| `src/hooks/input/build.ts` | A fact the event does not have is dropped with a warning, and a fact set to `undefined` is the same as one not set; a fact named like a common field never overrides the session's value (the facts of `SubagentStart` and `SubagentStop` own `agent_id` and `agent_type`); a fact with the wrong type or value is an error issue and no input is built | The docs describe what Claude Code sends, not what the kit does with a host's mistaken facts |

| `src/hooks/input/facts-shared.ts` | `context_tokens` (`SessionStart`, `PreModelSwitch`, `PostModelSwitch`) is a non-negative integer; the other numbers the docs type only as "number" (`duration_ms`, `seconds_since_last_response`, `estimated_cache_write_usd`) accept any number | The tables say "number" and "Tokens"; a token count is whole and never negative, a duration or a price is not constrained by the docs |

| `src/hooks/handlers/command/cwd-fallback.ts` | The directory the session started in and the system temp directory come from the host (`RunContext.startDirectory`, `tempDirectory`); when none of the candidates exists the process is started in the current directory and the port reports that it could not start | The kit has no clock, disk or temp-folder knowledge (L3); the docs name the four fallbacks but not the case where all are gone |
| `src/hooks/handlers/command/async.ts` | A stderr of only whitespace counts as empty for the `asyncRewake` wake text (stdout is used); when that text is blank too (exit code 2 with no output, or only whitespace) Claude is not woken; a process killed without a timeout (an abort) is a `cancelled` run | The docs say "stderr, or stdout if stderr is empty"; a blank reminder helps nobody, and the docs' log lists `cancelled` as an outcome |

| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/mcp-tool.ts` | `WAITS_FOR_SERVER` sorts all 33 events: an event waits when exit code 2 can stop what it guards or its output decides a result (`PermissionRequest`, `PermissionDenied`, `PostToolUse`, `MessageDisplay`); `SessionStart`, `Setup`, `SubagentStart` and `PostToolUseFailure` do not. | `src/hooks/handlers/remote/mcp-tool.ts` | A server that is not connected after the wait, a call the client could not make and a tool that fails are one `mcp` run with `isError: true` and a text; the server name is passed as the hook writes it, never rewritten | The docs say a non-connected server and `isError` are non-blocking errors and that `server` is already the scoped name |
| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/mcp-input.ts` | A `${path}` is keys joined by dots (a number indexes an array); strings nested in objects and arrays are substituted too; the text a path brings in is not read again; a non-string value is written as text (an object as JSON); a path with no value is an empty string and a warning `hooks.<Event>.input`; text that is not a path (`${not a path}`, `${}`) is left alone; numbers, booleans, null and keys are unchanged | The docs show only `${tool_input.file_path}` in a string value |
| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/prompt-text.ts` | The hook input is appended after a blank line when the prompt has no `$ARGUMENTS`; every `$ARGUMENTS` is replaced; `\$ARGUMENTS` is literal text and does not count as the placeholder; replacement is one pass, so the input is never unescaped | The docs give the placeholder, the `\$` escape and the append, not the separator or the edge cases |

| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/agent.ts` | An `agent` hook's run gets the tools `Read`, `Grep` and `Glob`; the 50-turn limit is the port's | The docs say the subagent "can use tools like Read, Grep, and Glob" and "up to 50 turns" |

| `src/hooks/handlers/remote/concurrency.ts` | `runLimited` returns the results in the order of the handlers and starts the waiting hooks in that order | The docs say the rest start "as running ones finish", not in which order; the order of handlers keeps what merges them deterministic |

| `src/mods/chain/nesting.ts` | The nesting limit counts nested arrays and objects (a scalar is 0 levels, `[1]` is 1), 28000 are allowed and the 28001st is refused | reference.md says "28,000 levels. Past that, ..." without defining a level |
| `src/mods/chain/api-guard.ts` | A call refused for nesting rejects with `$.<namespace>.<method> argument: nesting deeper than 28000 levels is not supported`, even for a method that is synchronous | reference.md fixes the end of the message and "the call rejects" |

| `src/mods/events/prompt.ts` | The reason of a `drop` returned after the hook's `next(e)` was answered is refused as `a drop after its next() was answered cannot stop a prompt that already went through`; only the phrase `a drop after its next() was answered` is the docs' | events.md quotes the phrase as part of the message the hook fails with, not the whole message |
| `src/mods/events/reasons.ts` | A "character" of the 4,096-character reason limit is a code point, so the cut never splits a surrogate pair; the cut applies to each hook's own result (an outer hook that awaits `next` already sees the cut reason), and to a `.catch` handler's answer; no version switch exists, so a hook never fails for a long reason as it did before v2.1.292 | reference.md says "characters" and "a hook's `drop` reason", not how a character is counted or at which point the cut happens, and the kit implements the 2.1.319 behaviour only |
| `src/mods/events/tool-check-merge.ts` | A decision is stricter in the order allow, ask, deny: in plan mode, for a call that is not read-only (`e.isReadOnly` absent), a mod's answer stricter than or as strict as Claude Code's decision stands and a looser one is replaced by Claude Code's; with `e.ceiling` `ask` a mod's `allow` becomes `ask` in every mode, keeping its reason; the permission mode, `e.ceiling` and Claude Code's own decision are inputs the host gives | events.md says a hook "can tighten the decision `next(e)` returns but can't loosen it" and permissions.md that it "can't approve" such a call "though it can still block"; neither orders `ask` against `deny` |
| `src/mods/events/session.ts` | On `session.append` only `message.content` of the event a hook passes to `next` reaches Claude Code: every other change is ignored (the stored row is the row as fired with the last `content`), and nothing fails | reference.md says to pass "a changed `message.content`" to rewrite the row's text blocks or a `tool_result` block's `content`, and names no other change |
| `src/mods/events/skill-prompt-event.ts` | `e.pluginName` is set only when `source` is `plugin`; `e.frontmatter` is `{}` for a skill with no file or no frontmatter; `e.frontmatterProblem` is the frontmatter parser's problem text (`is a list or a single value, not a table`, or the YAML error), so a table that parses is never partial | reference.md says "missing the file's frontmatter or one of its keys"; the kit's parser yields the whole table or none, and the keys a skills loader drops are the skills part's to report |
| `src/mods/events/autocomplete.ts` | `autocompleteRows` puts Claude Code's own rows first and the mods' rows after them, and an answer of the wrong shape adds none; that Claude Code "doesn't wait for the hook" is the host's to implement when it fires the event | reference.md fixes the order ("below Claude Code's own") and that the hook is not waited for, which is when the host asks the chain, not a behaviour of the chain |
| `src/mods/chain/rules.ts`, `src/mods/types.ts` | `EventRules` is the seam between the chain and the events: `shapeProblem` (told whether the hook had already called `next`), `changeProblem`, `coreInput` and `settle`; a hook's result is judged and settled where it is returned, and Claude Code's behaviour receives `coreInput(original, passed)` | The docs describe behaviours of events (a refused change, a resumed run that ignores one, a cut reason), not an engine; the chain stays free of per-event knowledge |
| `src/mods/events/session.ts` | A message that is not delivered makes Claude Code's own behaviour (`core`) reject, and the rejection reaches the hook's `await next(e)` like any other: a hook that does not catch it is skipped (`threw Error: ...`) and one with a `.catch` handler can answer; the chain never turns a resolved `{ isDelivered: false }` into a rejection, and a session that refuses inbound messages never reaches `session.receive`, which is the host's | api.md says "The hook's `next(e)` rejects when the message isn't delivered" and that a refusing session never shows the hook the message; it names neither the error nor who rejects |
| `src/mods/events/agent.ts` | The text Claude reads for a refused resumed run (`a plugin declined the resume:` followed by the hook's reason) is the host's: the kit returns the `{ deny: reason }` as answered and builds no sentence from it; and the hooks after a hook that changed a resumed run's event still see the change, only Claude Code's own behaviour receives the event as fired | reference.md says Claude "reads `a plugin declined the resume:` followed by your reason as that call's result" and that Claude Code "ignores the change", not whether later hooks see it |
| `src/mods/events/index.ts` | `eventInputProblem` is the kit's own helper and no chain rule calls it: the chain never refuses an event for the shape of its input; the host decides what to do with the problem it names | reference.md names the fields of each event's input and says nothing of an input that does not fit them, since Claude Code is what fires the events |
| `src/mods/events/prompt.ts`, `command.ts`, `config.ts`, `attribution.ts`, `session.ts`, `skill.ts` | Shape-only ids, proven by showing the documented answer is accepted and a wrong one refused, and that `next(e)` hands the event on as fired: `mods.event.prompt.compose`, `prompt.context`, `attribution.text`, `command.describe`, `config.describe`, `session.start`, `session.attach`, `session.detach`, `session.measure` and the `source` and `path` fields of `skill.prompt`; what Claude Code does with them is the host's | The docs give these events one answer shape or only `next(e)`, so the kit has no behaviour of its own beyond that shape |
| `src/mods/api/call.ts` | A mods API call resolves to its event's own result for `tool.call`, `tool.check`, `command.run`, `prompt.submit`, `prompt.fill`, `prompt.suggest`, `session.send` and `agent.spawn`; every other call answers `{ value }` or `{ deny }`, and `{ deny }` rejects with `<calling mod>: $.<namespace>.<method>: <reason>`; on a call whose event Claude Code also fires (`config.set`, `session.compact`...) an answer that is neither resolves as it is; a call with an argument of the wrong kind rejects `<label>: <issue>` (`$.fs.read: Invalid input: expected string, received number`), the first problem found; the event fields of `env.get` are `{ name }`, `env.set` `{ name, value }`, `config.set` `{ key, value }` and `tool.check` `{ tool, input }`; the call reaches the hooks of the mods that run before the caller (by tier, then as they registered) and its own, which are skipped as a re-entry, and a mod not registered yet is last of its tier | test.md lists `$.command.run`, `$.tool.call`, `session.send` and `prompt.fill` as taking the event's result and every other call as `{ value }` or `{ deny }`; reference.md says a hook "intercepts calls from the mods that run after it" but only `ui.notify` has its misuse message quoted; the rest of the split, the rejection wording, the overlapping events and the field names of those four events are not given |
| `src/mods/api/rules.ts` | A hook on a mods API call that no event of Claude Code's shares the name of (`fs.read`, `store.get`...) fails like a throw when it answers neither `{ value }`, `{ deny }` nor `next(e)` (a hook on the stream `process.spawn` answers what `next(e)` does, the command's own result, and only its `{ deny }` is special); the wording `returned neither { value } nor { deny }` is test.md's, the rest is the kit's | reference.md says such a hook "can return `next(e)`, `{ deny: reason }`, or `{ value }`" and test.md quotes the message for a stub, not what a hook that breaks the rule suffers |
| `src/mods/api/ports.ts` | The ports behind the API: `ModFs` (with `ancestors`, `stat` carrying `{ kind, size, isLink }`), `ModSpawner` (chunks `{ stream, text }`), `ModFetcher` (any method, no redirect followed, the kit judges each hop), `ModModels`, `ModSession`, `ModPrompts`, `ModAgents`, `ModCommands`, `ModTools`, `ModConfig`, `ModSettingsReader`, `ModAudio`, `ModEnv` and the shared ports; the host adapts its own runtime to them | The docs describe what each call does, not an embedding API; `HttpClient` takes `GET` and `POST` only and could not carry `HEAD`, so `$.http.fetch` has a port of its own |
| `src/mods/api/fs.ts`, `paths.ts` | A missing file rejects `<path>: no such file` and a missing directory `<path>: no such directory`; a file of more than 4 MiB rejects `<path>: <n> bytes is over the 4194304-byte limit` on read (the size the host reports) and on write (before the port is reached, counted in UTF-8 bytes); a relative path is joined to the working directory with `Locations.joinPath` and `~` is not expanded; `fs.ancestors` is the host's, and a `list` entry and a `stat` result carry only `{ name, kind, size, isLink }` and `{ kind, size, isLink }` whatever else the host puts on them | reference.md gives 4 MiB "for one file" and api.md says a relative path resolves against the working directory; no message, no rule for `~`, nothing for `ancestors` or `stat` |
| `src/mods/api/exec.ts` | A `timeoutMs` over 10 minutes is cut to 10 minutes; a program that cannot start rejects `<program>: <error>`, one still running at the timeout `<program> was still running after <n> ms`, one stopped before it exited `<program> was stopped before it exited`; the 4 MiB output cut counts UTF-8 bytes and drops a half character; `$.process.spawn` returns the stream of the `process.spawn` event with chunks `{ stream, text }`, and a refusal before the command started rejects `<mod>: $.process.spawn: <reason>` | reference.md says "10 minutes at most" and api.md quotes only the two `$.process.spawn started/ran, and a plugin withheld its result:` strings |
| `src/mods/api/http.ts`, `http-redirects.ts` | The event of `http.fetch` is `{ url, method, headers, body }` with `method` defaulting to `GET` and upper-cased and `headers` lower-cased; the response's header names are lower-cased by the kit and its `text` is cut to 4 MiB of UTF-8 bytes whatever the port sent; a redirect turns `POST` into `GET` on 301 and 302 and anything but `HEAD` into `GET` on 303 and keeps method and body on 307 and 308 (the fetch standard); a body is counted in code points; a URL that is not `http` or `https`, too many redirects and a timeout reject with `$.http.fetch: ...`; each request gets what is left of the 30 seconds; a response's `ok` is a 2xx status | api.md and reference.md give the five redirects, the dropped headers, the limits and the quoted Content-Length reason, not the method rule on a redirect or the other messages |
| `src/mods/api/model.ts`, `model-blocks.ts` | The event holds `maxTokens` already resolved to its default (1024); `maxTokens` above 64,000 is cut to 64,000 and the model's own output limit is the host's; `effort` is one of the SDK's `EffortLevel`s and another word rejects; `$.model.fork` takes `{ prompt }` as a string or blocks like `complete`; `$.model.classify` hands one object to the host | reference.md says "up to 64,000 or the model's output limit" and names `classify` without a signature; the event fields beyond `prompt`, `system`, `promptBlocks`, `systemBlocks` are not named |
| `src/mods/api/session.ts`, `prompt.ts` | `$.session.send` takes `{ sessionId }`, `{ agentId }` or a string and the event's `e.to` is that id as a string; `$.prompt.submit` passes the port `from: <mod>` or, for `asUser: true`, `asUser` and no `from` (`asUser: false` is a plain submit): the sentence that names the sender is the host's; `fill` and `suggest` take a string or `{ text }` | api.md says Claude reads text after a sentence that names the mod as the sender and test.md that `e.to` is a string; neither gives the sentence or the argument forms |
| `src/mods/api/command.ts`, `tool.ts`, `agent.ts`, `registries.ts` | `$.command.register`, `$.tool.register` and `$.agent.register` take an object with `name` (a subagent type has `name`, `description`, `prompt`); a command name another mod holds is refused `"/x" refused: it is already registered by <mod>`; the same mod registering again replaces; `list` returns what mods registered (a tool under its full name), not the host's own; unloading forgets a mod's registrations; the wording of the invalid-name refusal (`<what> name "x" is not valid: use letters, digits, _ and -, up to 64 characters`); a row of `$.agent.list()` whose `status` the reference does not list makes the call reject; `$.audio.play` takes one object and hands it on | api.md quotes the built-in refusal only and gives the command and tool registrations; `$.agent.register` has no example; what `list` covers is not said |
| `src/mods/api/store.ts` | A value that is not JSON (a cycle, a function, a bigint) rejects `$.store.set: the value is not JSON`, and a value is stored as JSON writes it (a property that is `undefined` is left out); a store that would pass 4 MiB of JSON (UTF-8 bytes of the whole object) rejects `$.store: the store would pass 4194304 bytes of JSON` and keeps what it had | interface.md says `set` "takes any JSON value" and reference.md gives "4 MiB of JSON in total"; neither says what happens past it |
| `src/mods/api/state.ts`, `state-library.ts` | `$.state.get` and `set` take `{ plugin, key }`; the store notifies on every read and write (`onRead`, `onWrite`) and `reset()` is what `/clear`, `/resume` and `/branch` call; `atom`, `read` and `update` are offered, `derive` and `memberOf` are not | interface.md names the five helpers and describes three; reactive tracking is the interface part of mods' to use |
| `src/mods/api/clock.ts` | A timer's callback that throws or rejects is logged as `<mod>: $.clock.every callback threw: <message>` (or `after`); `every` arms the next interval before the callback runs; an unloaded module cancels its timers; `after` and `every` return the timer at once, a misuse throws at the call, the timer is armed once its event has been through the chain (a `cancel()` before then keeps it from being armed), and a hook that refuses or answers in its place arms nothing and the reason goes to the debug log | api.md says the error goes to the debug log and the timer runs again, and test.md calls `timer.cancel()` on the return value with no `await`; no wording, no order of re-arming, and no word on a refused timer |
| `src/mods/api/mcp.ts` | `$.mcp.call` takes `{ server, tool, input }` and rejects when the server is not connected; `$.mcp.connect(server)` waits up to 30 seconds and resolves `{ isConnected }`, and rejects for a server the plugin's manifest does not list | reference.md says `connect` connects "an MCP server that your own plugin's manifest lists" and api.md that `call` calls a tool on a connected server; no signature, no result, no wait |
| `src/mods/api/telemetry.ts` | A `$.telemetry` call by a mod that is not built into Claude Code resolves and fires no event; the event for one that is carries `{ to: 'collector', record }` or `{ to: 'collector', feature }`, and a hook that answers `{ deny }` keeps the record from being sent and rejects the call | reference.md says "A record is sent only when Claude Code or a built-in mod makes the call" and requires `{ to: 'collector' }`; it names no other field |
| `src/mods/api/settings.ts`, `engine-settings.ts` | The merge of layers is the host's (`ModSettingsReader.merged`); `key` is not a field of the event; a `source` the reference does not list rejects; on Claude Code's own read a key the hook leaves out is unset and one it changes that fails the schema check (`fits`, injected: the settings stream owns the schemas) keeps its merged value (so does an answer JSON cannot write), with the two debug lines quoted in troubleshoot.md | events.md gives the layers, the answers and the two log lines; the precedence merge and the schema of each key belong to the settings stream |
| `src/mods/api/engine-create.ts`, `src/mods/api/scoped-call.ts` | The `engine.create` event is `{ plugin }` and its answer an object of namespaces: one it adds is kept, one it changes replaces the built one, one it leaves out comes back unless the answering mod may withhold (`canWithhold`, the host says whether a mod outside the `user` tier answers); a method of an added namespace runs every other mod's calls in the caller's context (working directory, origin, a `lent` re-entry) while it runs, and `$.prompt.submit` then rejects `the turn is waiting on a hook of <mod>`; calls that overlap from two mods can cross contexts | reference.md gives the event, "outside the `user` tier can also withhold" and the lent-context paragraph, not the event's fields, who answered, how a method learns whose `$` it uses, or the wording of the rejection |
| `src/mods/api/build.ts` | Without the interface part of mods every `$.ui` call rejects `$.ui.<method>: the interface is not available`, except `$.ui.resolve`, which throws the same message at once because it answers without a promise; `$` is frozen and holds the namespaces in the reference's order; every method of the API is asynchronous except the values `$.plugin.name` and `$.plugin.root`, `$.process.spawn`, which returns the stream, and `$.clock.after` and `$.clock.every`, which return the timer | The docs show `await` on the calls and `$.plugin.name` as a value; the interface part is a later sub-package |
| `src/protocol/mods-ui-names.ts` | A render site whose props the table gives in words (`AssistantMessage`, `ToolUse`, `ToolResult`, `ToolGroup`, `AskUserQuestion`) has an empty list of named props, so a test proves only its component, app and request id; a site with "One instance" has no `requestId` in its event | The table names no field for those rows, and names no id for an instance; the complete reference is `claude-code.d.ts`, which is not in the pulled docs |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/box.ts` | A `Box` takes the flexbox layout props `flexWrap`, `flexGrow`, `flexShrink`, `flexBasis`, `alignItems`, `alignSelf` and `justifyContent` beside `flexDirection`; a spacing value that is not a number of cells (0 or more) or a step name fails the tree | The table says "flex layout" without listing it, and only `flexDirection`'s four values are quoted |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/validate.ts`, `src/mods/ui/elements/*.ts` | An `Svg` `source` over 131,072 characters fails the tree, an `Input` `rows` that is not a whole number of 1 or more fails it (`Input prop "rows" must be a whole number, 2 or more for several lines`), and a `Link` with no `href` fails it (`Link needs an href`); a prop outside an element's list fails the tree (`<Element> prop "x" is not allowed`, quoted for `Text`); the wording of every other check (`must be a string`, `is longer than N characters`, `takes no children`, `is not drawn by the <app> app`, `needs at least one option`) is the kit's | The docs say "a prop an element doesn't take or a child where none goes" fails the hook and quote two lines; the lists are the Elements table's main props; the reference gives the `Svg` source as "up to" 131,072 characters and says nothing of `rows` below 1 or of a missing `href` |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/children.ts` | A `Text` holds strings and `Text`; a `Box` holds strings, elements and Claude Code's own drawing; `Button` holds strings and `Text` (documented); every other element holds nothing | The docs state the `Button` rule and `Markdown`'s `text`, and name no other |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/button.ts`, `validate.ts` | `variant` and `role` throw when an element function is called (documented "throws"), with the kit's wording; `autoFocus: false`, a bad `hotkey` and two `Select`s with one key fail the tree instead of throwing; two `Button`s with one key are a valid tree and only a press by that key fails (`key each once`) | The docs say the variant and role throw, that `autoFocus` "accepts only `true`", that `Input`/`Select` keys are drawn twice, and that a duplicate button key is an error "from `press` in a test" |

| `src/mods/ui/elements/limits.ts` | A text child is one level and one node; the text of a tree is its string children; an `Image` source that is a `Uint8Array` counts its bytes and a string (a file path) counts none; `Raster` `columns` above 512, `rows` above 256 and `Image` above 255 fail the hook, while the other size limits refuse the tree at its site; the refusal texts other than `deeper than 32; nothing was drawn` are the kit's | The docs quote one refusal text and list the limits without saying which are element rules |
| `src/mods/ui/sites/fold.ts`, `sites/registry.ts` | The refusal line starts `hooks: ` when more than one mod has a hook for the site (the host passes `mods`); an `AskUserQuestion` tree without exactly one reference leaves the dialog to Claude Code; the reference's `ref` is opaque; the VS Code extension needs a viewport, the others have none until measured | The docs quote both lines and the rule, and say the viewport is "absent until the app has measured its window" |
| `src/mods/ui/sites/band.ts`, `rules.ts` | A band tree is checked for the forbidden props on every `Box` around any tree an earlier-registered hook settled on or Claude Code's reference; the hook that returned it fails as the wrong shape, so the other mods' bands stay; the wording is the kit's | The docs say the tree is invalid and the others stay, and list the props |
| `src/mods/chain/failure.ts`, `src/mods/types.ts` | A hook whose result has the wrong shape is skipped with `returned the wrong shape (<rule>)` (the documented line); only a result nested too deep is `refused`; `EventRules.shapeProblem` receives the event as fired so a tree is judged for its site and app | troubleshoot.md quotes `ui.render hook skipped: returned the wrong shape (a tree Pane refuses: ...)`; the earlier text did not match it |
| `src/mods/types.ts` | `$.ui.resolve(e)` answers at once with the element table, not a promise | The docs destructure its result without `await`, and the table was resolved when mods loaded |
| `src/mods/ui/tables.ts` | `ui.resolve` fires once per mod for each of the three apps and 15 sites with `{ plugin, surface, component }`; a hook's answer must be a record of element functions | The docs give the three axes and say the result is the element table; they name no field |
| `src/mods/ui/controls/route.ts` | A typed value or a pick is in `e.value` of `ui.input` and `ui.select`, and `ui.press` carries `surface`; typing with Enter reaches `onSubmit`, typing alone `onInput`; a callback that throws is an issue, not a throw | The docs say a hook "sees what the user typed ... and can change that text" and that a press callback gets `e.surface`, without naming the field |
| `src/mods/ui/runtime.ts` | `ui.message` carries `{ data }` and, like `ui.fault`, reaches only the hooks of the mod that drew the `Client` (`DispatchOptions.only`); `ui.focus` and `ui.scroll` carry what the host gives (`{ requestId, key }` and `{ requestId, offset }` in the tests are the kit's wording) and `change` applies the result, and a hook that answers without calling `next` keeps the change from happening; a failed `Client` is replaced by a dimmed `Text` (`dimColor`) holding the documented line (`clientFailure`); `ui.fault` redraws the sites directly (not through the throttle) after its hooks return | The docs name `e.phase` and `e.reason` for `ui.fault` and say the hook runs once more; nothing else about these events |
| `src/mods/ui/panes/placement.ts`, `registry.ts` | The 144/110 column rule is the terminal's; a pane a mod opens by itself waits when the terminal is not measured; the other apps place it; a placed pane with `holdToasts` holds toasts in the terminal only (`PaneInfo.holdsToasts`); `rows` and `columns` are whole numbers of 1 or more; `$.ui.panes()` gives `{ id, title, isPlaced }` | The docs say "terminal" and describe the rule by that; they name no behaviour without a measure and no shape for the list |

| `src/mods/ui/toast.ts`, `ui-api.ts` | A toast with a bad `timeoutMs` is dropped with the debug line and the call resolves; a toast's time runs from when it is raised and stands still while a hold of toasts lasts, so a hold longer than the timeout does not use the toast up; `$.ui.toast` with text that is not a string is dropped with `text is a string`; the classic renderer shows one at a time and fullscreen three; `$.ui.notice`, `blit`, `focus` and `scroll` pass their argument to the host as given; `$.ui.close` is the `ui.close` event with `origin.kind` `plugin` | The docs give the debug lines, the default and the limits; they name no arguments for those calls |
| `src/mods/ui/redraw/subscribe.ts` | The `$.state` reads made while one site is drawn subscribe that site, replacing what it read last time; reads of sites drawn at once are not told apart (each subscribes to both, which only redraws more); a write of a read value asks for a redraw through the throttle, except a write of a value the drawing in progress has itself read (a `ui.render` hook "can read state and can't write it", and redrawing for its write would loop) |  interface.md says a `ui.render` hook that reads a value subscribes to it and writing redraws "that site"; it says nothing of concurrent draws, the throttle or what a write inside the hook does |

| `src/mods/ui/sites/registry.ts` | The kit passes the props the host gives and does not model that `UserMessage` carries `task` or `from` by origin, nor the fields of the `Pane` `view` and `scroll` props | The reference names the props in words, and the host knows the origin |

| `src/mods/load/plugin-dirs.ts` | The directories of `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` are trimmed, empty pieces are dropped, relative paths are accepted, a directory named twice loads once, flags come before the variable, and the process environment wins over `env` in settings | The docs give "absolute paths separated by `:`" and say the directories load "as `--plugin-dir` does"; the rest is how a list of paths is read sensibly |
| `src/mods/load/plugin-dirs.ts` | The host exposes `pluginDirs()` (nothing once startup is refused) and `watchesPluginDirs(isInteractive)`; several rejected flags are one error of one line each, in the order the flags and then the variable | The host is where the policy and the directories meet; the docs say the error names the rejected flags and the variable, not how several are joined |

| `src/mods/load/scan/file-rules.ts` | `require()` is refused with `require() is not available; write every file as an ES module with import`; an import that climbs out of the plugin directory (`../../x.js` from `hooks/`) is refused with `imports "<specifier>", outside the plugin directory` | create.md says "Write every file as an ES module, with `import` and not `require`" and "Import only from files inside the plugin directory" and gives no message |
| `src/mods/load/scan/rules.ts` | A name read from the environment that is not a literal is labelled `(computed)`; a hook that is not a function the scan can read, such as one imported from another file, fails with `the hook passed to on("x") is not a function the scan can read` | The docs name what the scan reads (a function declared in the file) and say a computed name is marked; they do not word the failure |
| `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | The debug line of a loaded module reads `hooks module <id> loaded (tier <tier>); events: <list>`; a reload of one mod reads `<mod>: reloaded; hooks: <list>`, a failed one `<mod>: reload failed, the previous version stays loaded: <reason>`; a module that hooks `engine.create` needs the types file | troubleshoot.md quotes a line with the worker and its environment, which belong to the host's runtime; it names the failed-reload prefix only; the scan cannot tell a hook that adds a namespace from one that only withholds |
| `src/mods/load/host.ts` | The host fires `session.start` for each loaded mod after `load` and `reload`, and for one mod after `reloadMod`, with `{ surface, isInteractive, cwd }`; the test kit turns it off | reference.md says "once for each loaded mod ... and again after a reload of that mod" and test.md that the kit does not fire it; the fields are those the test.md example fires |
| `src/mods/load/register-call.ts` | `e` of `plugin.register` also carries `plugin`, the name of the mod about to load | The docs name `e.tier` and `e.uses`; a policy mod that names its refusals needs the mod |
| `src/testing/mods/mock-clock.ts` | A move of the clock lets the promises a timer started run for 64 turns, and runs at most 10,000 timers in one move | The docs say the methods "run each timer that comes due" and give no bound; a chain of timers must end |
| `src/testing/mods/world.ts`, `src/mods/load/unanswered.ts` | A mods API call that no stub and no mod before the caller answers fails with `no implementation for <name>`, at the end of the chain, except `ui.invalidate`, `$.state` and `session.append`; `session.start` is not fired by the kit | test.md lists the three the kit answers itself and the error text; where in the chain the failure sits is not said |
| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | The read-only forms of `git` are `status`, `log`, `diff` and `show`, with the subcommand directly after `git` | permissions.md lists "read-only forms of `git`" without naming them |
| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | Safe environment variables and process wrappers are not seen through for the read-only set (`timeout 5 ls` and `LANG=C ls` prompt); they are for the `acceptEdits` commands, as documented | The docs give the wrapper rule only for `acceptEdits`, so the stricter reading is used for the read-only set. Any assignment (`LANG=C ls`) keeps a command out of the read-only set, which covers the documented special-variable rule (`PATH`, `IFS`) for a prefix or a standalone assignment; `for`, `unset`, `read` and `export` of a special variable are caught on their own (`read-only-set.ts`) |
| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | A redirect whose target is a file (not `/dev/null`, a descriptor form or a here-document) makes a command not read-only and not an `acceptEdits` command; redirect targets are not checked against Edit and Read rules | permissions.md, "Redirections", describes the target check; the conservative reading is a prompt |
| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | In an `acceptEdits` command every option word is an option and every other word a path; a glob, variable, substitution or unresolvable operand prompts; `sed`'s first operand is its script unless `-e`/`-f` is given; relative operands follow a `cd` earlier in the command | The docs name the commands and "paths inside your working directory", not how operands are read |
| `src/rules/decide/powershell-edits.ts` | PowerShell has no read-only set in `decide`. The `acceptEdits` cmdlets are `Set-Content`, `Add-Content`, `Clear-Content` and `Remove-Item` with PowerShell's built-in aliases (`sc`, `ac`, `clc`, `ri`, `rm`, `rmdir`, `rd`, `del`, `erase`); a call is approved only with the named parameters `-Path`, `-LiteralPath`, `-Value`, `-Encoding`, `-Force`, `-NoNewline`, `-WhatIf`, `-Confirm` (and `-Recurse` for `Remove-Item`), a literal path in scope, and no quote character in a positional argument; any other parameter, a variable or a wildcard prompts | permission-modes.md names the four cmdlets and "their common aliases" without listing them, and gives the quote-character rule only |
| `src/rules/decide/critical-removal.ts` | The "variable followed by a top-level directory name" form uses the four documented names (`mnt`, `tmp`, `usr`, `Users`); `rm logs/*/*` and `rm logs/*/` are the two documented "end in `/*` or `/*/`" shapes; a Windows path at depth two or less is critical; an `sh -c` script is read when it is the argument of `-c` | The table says "such as" and "Some targets"; the rest of the `rm` check is not given |
| `src/rules/decide/auto-dropped.ts` | Auto mode drops allow rules for wildcarded `python`, `python3`, `node`, `ruby` and `perl` and for `npm`, `yarn`, `pnpm` and `bun` `run` commands; `PowerShell(*)` counts as the blanket rule | The docs name "wildcarded interpreters like `Bash(python*)`" and "Package-manager run commands" without a list |
| `src/rules/decide/modes.ts` | A file edit that no rule approves asks in plan mode (reason `mode-plan`), not a denial | The docs say edits "stay blocked until you approve the plan" and, for a hook allow, "the same prompt, block, or approval the call would get with no hook" |
| `src/rules/decide/modes.ts` | In plan mode an allow rule approves what it covers, as in `default` | The docs layer rules on the baseline of every mode and say only that hook and mod approvals do nothing in plan mode |
| `src/rules/decide/modes.ts` | A critical-path removal in `bypassPermissions` mode is `ask` (the host's prompt, callback or `PermissionRequest` hook decides); in auto mode it is `ask` at a terminal and `deny` elsewhere | The table differs per place (VS Code panel, desktop, `-p`); the kit knows only whether the session is interactive |
| `src/rules/decide/decide.ts` | A hook allow does not approve a protected-path write, as an allow rule does not; a mod approval does not approve a critical-path removal, a protected-path write, a network read or a tool that requires user interaction | The docs say none of these for hooks and mods beyond the cases they list; "no mode auto-approves" is read as no approval |
| `src/rules/decide/decide.ts` | A hook `defer` is no answer: the call takes the normal permission flow | hooks.md says `defer` is honoured only with `-p` and ends the turn, which the host does |

| `src/rules/decide/classifier.ts` | With no `PermissionClassifier` a `classify` decision is settled as a denial | permission-modes.md: with no verdict "Claude Code denies the action rather than run it unreviewed" |

| `src/rules/decide/read-only-set.ts` | The special shell variables are the two the docs name (`PATH`, `IFS`) and the kit's seed (`CDPATH`, `ENV`, `BASH_ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, `PS4`, `PROMPT_COMMAND`, `GLOBIGNORE`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH`); a match anywhere in the command text, quoted text included, makes it not read-only | permissions.md says "certain special shell variables, such as `PATH` or `IFS`" |
| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | `cd -` and a bare `cd` are not read-only (the previous directory is unknown, and `~` is outside the working directories); a `cd` with an unresolvable operand prompts | permissions.md reads a `cd` only by its target path |

| `src/rules/decide/shell-commands.ts` | A path the host's symlink check resolved (`PreparedCall.resolvedPaths`) applies to every filesystem command and file-reading command of a Bash call, not to the operand it came from | The host's list carries no link to an operand |
| `src/rules/decide/facts.ts` | A command that does not parse gets the critical-path outcome of the mode (`Dangerous rm operation not ruled out`); "the shell may read this command differently than it was parsed" is read as the kit's own unparseable commands | permission-modes.md names the prompt but not which constructs trigger it |
| `src/rules/decide/facts.ts` | `LSP` is not covered by the read block: the path an LSP call names is not a field the docs give | settings-reference.md lists `LSP` among the refused tools; `decide` cannot read its target |

| `src/rules/decide/protected-paths.ts` | Any file under `~/.claude/plans` and any `~/.claude/jobs/<id>/tmp` is an own file; the kit cannot tell the session's own plan file or scratch directory from another session's | permission-modes.md says "the current session's own"; the kit has no session identity |
| `src/rules/decide/protected-paths.ts` | `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is the host's `ProtectedPathOptions.configDirectory` and stands in for `~/.claude` in the plan, jobs and memory exceptions and for `dev-mods`; the stored server-managed settings and their signature and consent files are protected only by the name `remote-settings.json` | The kit reads no environment; the docs do not name the other files |
