| `src/rules/decide/critical-removal.ts` | After a `cd` earlier in the command, any relative target ending in `/*` is critical; a `~N` name beside the home directory is its 8.3 short name on Windows; the Windows spellings `\\?\C:\` and `\\localhost\C$` are read as `C:\`; a target of only backslashes, and a `\\?\Volume{...}` path, are critical on every platform. | `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | `availableTools` takes the candidate tools from the host and filters them; the model, provider and surface conditions (task tools by model, `EndConversation`'s surface and provider) are the host's facts | The docs give model and provider lists that change per release; the kit holds no model table |
| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | `--tools` restricts built-in tools only; an MCP tool stays in the list | The table says "restricts the session's built-in tools to the ones it names" and says nothing of MCP tools |

| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | An allow rule saves `EndConversation` from `*` only when it names it exactly (bare, no glob, no specifier) | The docs say "unless an allow rule names `EndConversation` explicitly" |
| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | `--restricted` removes `Bash`, `PowerShell`, `Monitor` and `WebFetch` unless `--tools` names them; `Workflow` stays | cli-reference.md says "the built-in tools that run commands or code, and WebFetch" without listing them; the first three run a command, and `Workflow` orchestrates subagents rather than running code itself |
| `src/rules/tools/availability.ts` | The candidates a host passes already include the task tools (`TaskCreate`, `TaskGet`, `TaskList`, `TaskUpdate`, `TodoWrite`) its model offers and the opt-ins of `--allowedTools`, `--tools` and `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS`; `availableTools` never adds a tool | The default set depends on a model list that changes per release, and the kit holds no model table |
| `src/rules/tools/catalog.ts`, `src/rules/decide/facts.ts` | An `LSP` call has no documented input, so it is not read for an outside path and the table's No column holds for it | tools-reference.md says file-access tools marked No still prompt outside the working directories, but gives no input shape for `LSP` |

| `src/rules/tools/inputs/fields.ts` | A field's `role` is what a rule does with it (`primary`, `subject`, `param`, `structured`), and where the docs document it is a separate `source` (`sdk` or `docs`); `subject` is the Agent `subagent_type`, the one non-primary field a tool's own specifier reads | The docs list primary fields and "any scalar parameter" and say nothing of a "doc-only" kind; mixing documentation and behaviour in one value would make `WebSearch.mode` either matchable or doc-only |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/fields.ts` | Optionality where the table is silent follows the SDK input type: Agent `subagent_type` is optional, Agent `prompt` and `description` and WebFetch `prompt` are required; Bash and PowerShell `run_in_background`, Edit `replace_all` and Grep `-i` and `multiline` are optional; ExitPlanMode's three fields are optional (the model's literal input "is typically empty") | The Agent, WebFetch, Bash, PowerShell, Edit and Grep tables carry no "Optional" marker for these rows ("Whether to run the command in background", "Case insensitive search"); the SDK types say which are required |

| `src/rules/tools/inputs/ask-user-question.ts` | `answerQuestions` takes its input type from the SDK's `AskUserQuestionInput` with `multiSelect` optional | hooks.md says "optional `multiSelect` flag"; the SDK types it as required, and a hook's input may leave it out |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/fields.ts` | Agent `model` is any string, not the SDK's four aliases; `Grep.output_mode` is the three values of the table; no number is range-checked (`timeout`, `offset`, `limit`) | The docs say "model alias" and show that a full model ID can be sent (`Agent(model:opus)` "but not a full model ID"); a Bash timeout above the maximum "is reduced ... rather than rejected" |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/parse-input.ts` | `parseToolInput` reports every problem as an `error` at `tool_input.<field>` (`is required`, `expected a string`); a field the table does not list and a tool without a table pass through; an input that is not an object is one issue at `tool_input` and comes back as `{}` | The docs say what a field is, not what the kit says when one is wrong; the SDK's input types carry fields the tables leave out (Bash `dangerouslyDisableSandbox`) |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/ask-user-question.ts` | A question has 2 to 4 options (the SDK type); an option has a `label`, a `description` and an optional `preview`; the 12-character limit of `header` is not enforced | The table says "options array" and "short `header`"; the counts and the limit are in the SDK's types and comments only |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/ask-user-question.ts` | `answerQuestions` refuses a selection for a question that was not asked and two selections for a single-select question (an issue at `tool_input.answers.<question>`, nothing added), leaves a question with no selection unanswered, keeps a text that is not an option label, and keeps answers already in the input | The docs give the shape of `answers` and the comma join, not the cases around it; the SDK says no "Other" option is listed because it "will be provided automatically", so a free text is a legitimate answer |

| `src/rules/tools/inputs/web-search.ts` | A `mode` that is neither `standard` nor `extended` is an issue in `parseToolInput` and reads as absent (the full search) in `effectiveSearchMode` | The docs name the two values and the default of an absent `mode`, not an unknown one |
| `src/rules/tools/inputs/tool-path.ts` | `expandToolPath` folds `.` and `..` in absolute paths too, expands only `~` and `~/`, writes a Windows drive in upper case and keeps a UNC path's two backslashes; `expandToolInputPath` expands only the `file_path` of `Write`, `Edit` and `Read` | The docs say `~` and relative paths are expanded and the Windows path has backslashes; they do not say how `..`, `~user` or a UNC path read, and name only those three tools |
| `src/rules/param.ts` | `NotebookEdit`'s `notebook_path` is a primary field that comes from permissions.md, not from a table: hooks.md has no `tool_input` table for it, so it stays a separate entry beside the `primary` roles of `TOOL_INPUT_FIELDS` | permissions.md lists it with the other primary fields; nothing documents its other fields |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/edit.ts` | An empty `old_string` is refused; occurrences are counted left to right without overlap (`aa` in `aaa` is one); the wording of the refusals (not found, found N times, empty) | The docs say `old_string` must appear "exactly once" and give no empty or overlapping case, and quote no message |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/encoding.ts` | The wording of the Write refusal for `U+FFFD`; the guard applies only when Write replaces a file Edit would refuse (not a missing file, not valid UTF-8, not a UTF-16 little-endian file) | tools-reference.md says Write may replace "such a file" unless the content holds `U+FFFD`; it quotes no message and states no other case |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/env-number.ts` | A variable that is not a positive integer counts as unset; so does a `timeout` that is not a positive number (`effectiveBashTimeout`) and a `bashOutputMaxChars` that is not a positive integer | The docs give defaults and ceilings, not what a malformed value does |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/bash-exit.ts` | A compound line exits with its last command (the subcommand that ends the text); a command behind a wrapper, a variable assignment (`LC_ALL=C grep`), a subshell or a trailing `&`, or with options before the subcommand (`sudo grep`, `git -C . diff`), is read as written and is a failure; a command after `!` is a failure (the `!` inverts the status, so exit 1 means the command matched); any exit status but 0 and 1 is a failure; PowerShell lines are split as Bash lines are | The docs list the commands whose exit 1 is benign, not how a line of several commands, a negation, a prefix, a background or a wrapped command is read |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/bash-output.ts` | A failure's head-and-tail excerpt is half head, half tail, cut from the first `readBack` characters; a read-back window below 30,000 also lowers the valid inline ceiling | tools-reference.md says "a head-and-tail excerpt of that size cut from the read-back window" and does not split it or relate a small window to the inline ceiling |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/read-window.ts` | `offset` counts lines from 1; an `offset` or `limit` below 1 or not an integer is an issue at `tool_input.<field>` and counts as absent; a line ends at `\n` or `\r\n` and a final newline starts no line; the wording of the two notices | hooks.md gives `offset` 10 as "line number to start reading from" without a base and tools-reference.md names the notices without their text |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/web-fetch.ts` | Text that does not parse, or a scheme other than http and https, is an "Invalid URL" issue; a root-label dot does not make `localhost.` a dotted name; a bracketed address (`[::1]`) has no dot and is refused; the preapproved documentation domains are not modelled | errors.md names "Invalid URL" only as the old message for the refusals and tools-reference.md does not list the preapproved set |
| `src/rules/tools/semantics/web-fetch.ts` | `sliceFetched` counts code points; an offset past the end reads nothing; an offset that is not a whole number from 0 counts as absent; the wording of the unread notice | hooks.md says "characters to skip"; tools-reference.md says the result "tells Claude how much went unread" without a text |

| `src/format/mcp-sources.ts` | The wording of `mcpServers path not found: <path>` (after the load-time `<component> path not found: <path>`), `... path escapes plugin directory: <path>`, `... is not valid JSON`, `... must hold a JSON object of servers`, the bundle extension and `https://` messages, and the unusable-entry message; a source that fails does not stop the others; sources load concurrently and merge in order | The docs quote the load-time forms for a missing or escaping path and nothing for the rest; severity `error` for a component that does not load, as for any other component path |

| `src/format/mcp-bundles.ts` | No `McpBundleReader` is a warning `MCP bundle <path> needs an extractor: the host gave none`; a bundle the host does not find reuses `mcpServers path not found`; a failed opening is an error `MCP bundle <path> could not be opened: <message>`; `Bundled MCP server "<name>" was not started: it needs configuration` is an error issue (the Errors tab shows it) that drops only that server | The docs say a bundle needs an extractor only implicitly, and say the rest of the plugin works when a bundled server does not start |
| `src/loading/resolve/mcp.ts` | `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_NAME` is the registered `plugin:<plugin>:<server>` key and `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_URL` the substituted URL; `<plugin>` is the plugin identifier up to `@`; a URL that does not parse after substitution (an unset option, an unset `${VAR}`, a covered credential variable alone) keeps the server from starting with `Invalid MCP server config for "<server>": URL is unset or invalid`; a missing environment variable stays a warning | mcp.md says "the name of the MCP server" while the server registers under the scoped name; troubleshooting.md gives the URL cause for an unset option and mcp.md says a bad URL is reported, not when it is evaluated |
| `src/loading/resolve/shell-guard.ts` | `rejectUserConfigInShell` is generic over a `ShellGuard<Context>` (message only); the surface's resolver owns its guard and its context (`headersHelper` holds the server name); the resolver wraps the `headersHelper` message in `Invalid MCP server config for "<server>":` | errors.md quotes one message per surface and troubleshooting.md lists the headersHelper one as a cause after the colon of the wrapper |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp-runtime.ts` | **J-L2** `maxRestarts` unset means unbounded restarts while `restartOnCrash` is true; `restartsSoFar` counts restart attempts already made (not the crash in hand), so `maxRestarts: 2` restarts the first two crashes and stops at the third; `maxRestarts: 0` never restarts | The page says "Restart attempts before giving up, zero or more" and names no default or counting rule |
| `src/format/lsp-config.ts` | `command` is invalid when empty and when it holds any whitespace (not only a space) unless it starts with `/`; the rule is applied to the value as written, so `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/x` is valid and a Windows path with spaces is not; a language ID may be any string | The page says "No spaces unless the value starts with `/`"; nothing about empty values, tabs, variables or Windows |
| `src/format/lsp-config.ts` | An inline `lspServers` map and any declared `.json` file are read like `.lsp.json`: one invalid entry skips the whole map; the text after `Invalid LSP server config for "<origin>":` (`<server>: <field>: <reason>`) is the kit's; every invalid entry gets its own issue; a map that is not an object is one issue | components.md states the skip and the prefix for `.lsp.json` only; the manifest check rejects the plugin for a bad inline entry anyway |
| `src/format/lsp-config.ts` | `mergeLspSources` replaces a server whole (no field-wise merge) and keeps the first position of a name, so registration order is the order names first appeared | components.md says the manifest server "replaces" the `.lsp.json` one; "first registered" for extensions is not defined across a replacement |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp.ts` | `${user_config.KEY}` resolves only in `command`, `args`, `env` and `workspaceFolder`, not in `initializationOptions`, `settings` or `extensionToLanguage`; `${VAR}` is not expanded in LSP fields; unset `startupTimeout` and `maxRestarts` stay unset; `args` and `env` default to empty in the resolved view; the data folder is created for every LSP process | manifest-reference says "substituted in ... LSP server config" but its table of fields lists four, and the hard-requirement reading keeps the table; the docs give LSP no `${VAR}` expansion and no startup default |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp-runtime.ts` | `languageIdFor` matches the extension case-sensitively at the end of the last path segment (`/` or `\`), a name that is only the extension has none, the longest matching extension wins (`.d.ts` over `.ts`), the first registered server among equals; `didChangeConfigurationOf` returns nothing when `settings` is unset; `initializeOptionsOf` returns only the `initializationOptions` member, the host adds the rest of the request | The page says "Map of file extension to LSP language ID" and "the first registered handles those files"; case, multi-dot extensions and what an absent `settings` sends are open |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp-runtime.ts` | `languageIdFor` and `lspExtensionConflicts` read a record keyed by server name; across plugins the key is plugin-qualified (`plugin:server`), and the warning names that key (`LSP server "beta:go" is not used for .go files`); two plugins that declare the same bare name are not told apart by an unqualified record | components.md says the first registered server handles an extension "whether the servers come from one plugin or two" and the warning shows `<name>` without saying whether it is qualified |
| `src/format/lsp-config.ts`, `src/loading/resolve/lsp-runtime.ts` | Registration order is the key order of a plain object, so integer-like server names (`"9"`, `"10"`) are ordered ascending ahead of the others whatever their first appearance; the kit does not carry order beside the record | components.md defines "first registered" but not an order for such names; the names of a real language server are not integers |
| `src/loading/resolve/lsp.ts` | `shutdownTimeout` and `startupTimeout` are proven by the config reader (a positive integer) and by the resolved view (declared value kept, unset stays unset); terminating the process when `shutdownTimeout` elapses and waiting `startupTimeout` for startup are the host's LSP client, so no kit decision stands behind the declared-value tests | manifest-reference.md describes both as client behaviour on a process the kit never starts |
| `src/format/monitors.ts` | `name`, `command` and `description` are required, so an empty string counts as missing; the skill after `on-skill-invoke:` must not be empty and is kept as written (no trimming, no case folding, a qualified `plugin:skill` stays whole); any `when` string other than `always` or `on-skill-invoke:<skill>` (`ALWAYS`, `on-skill-invoke` with no colon) is an error that blocks loading; the source must be an array (a `{ monitors: [...] }` wrapper is an error); an issue's path is `<origin>[<index>]`; the wording of the messages | The table says the three fields are required and `when` has two forms, and names no other value, so what any other string does is open ("strict object" covers keys, not values); it gives no rule for empty values or the file wrapper; "a `.json` file holding the monitors array" names a bare array |
| `src/format/monitors.ts` | A repeated `name` is an error that blocks loading, and the later entry is left out (the first entry keeps the name); an invalid entry is left out and reported as an error too, while the valid entries are still returned | The docs say only "Identifier unique within the plugin" for `name`, with no error, no "plugin doesn't load" and no winner for a repeat; only an unknown key is documented as an error ("the plugin doesn't load"); the docs give no partial result either; the caller blocks loading on the errors, and one that reports and carries on has the rest |

| `src/loading/resolve/monitor-trigger.ts` | **J-L10** `started` holds the names of this plugin's monitors the host has running or has started; a monitor in it is never returned again, `always` ones too (a reload does not start a second copy), and a host that stopped a monitor removes its name to have a reload start it again | The docs fix "the first time that skill runs" for skill monitors, and say that disabling a plugin does not stop running monitors; they do not say what a reload does to a running `always` monitor |

| `src/loading/resolve/content.ts` | A command body takes the same `user_config` policy as a skill or agent body: a non-sensitive value substitutes and a sensitive one becomes `SENSITIVE_PLACEHOLDER` | The `${user_config.KEY}` bullet of "Reference a saved value" names only "skill and agent content", while the "Where each variable resolves" table has one row, "Skill, command, and agent content", and the docs give commands no separate rule, so the kit reads the row as covering all three |
| `src/loading/load-plugin.ts` | `loadPlugin` returns no plugin only when `plugin.json` is not JSON or fails validation, or when there is neither a manifest nor a `fallbackName` (or that name may not be a plugin name); any other error issue (a missing or escaping path, an invalid hooks, LSP or monitors file, a bad `settings.json`) skips that component and the plugin loads, with the issue at its own severity | The docs say a rejected path drops only that component and an invalid `.lsp.json` is skipped as a file, and that a manifest with an error "doesn't load"; they name no result shape for the other strict-object errors, so a host that wants to refuse such a plugin uses `blocksLoading(issues)` |

| `src/format/plugin-settings.ts` | A settings key other than `agent` and `subagentStatusLine`, and a value of the wrong shape for one of them, is dropped with a warning; a `settings.json` that is not an object is an error; a `settings.json` that is not valid JSON is an error and the manifest's `settings` still applies | The docs say other keys are "dropped" and name no message, no shape check and no failure case |

| `src/host/session-skills.ts` | A session with no `permissions` answer treats every shell placeholder as `ask`, which aborts the expansion; the shells default to `bash`, plus PowerShell on a Windows host | The docs describe the permission prompt, which a host without one cannot show; which shells exist is the machine's, which the kit cannot see |

| `src/settings/general/checks.ts` | A general key with a value of the wrong type is dropped with the warning `"<key>" must be <what>; the key is ignored` (the one of errors.md for `crossSessionInbound`); a list entry that is not a string, or a permission rule that does not have the shape `Tool` or `Tool(content)`, is skipped on its own with the errors.md message; in the policy tier an unreadable `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` reads as `true`; the other restrictive general keys are not read as locks, and the per-field repairs of `permissions` in managed settings (managed-settings.md, "Keys that fail closed") are not made | settings.md says an entry that fails is skipped and the rest of the file stays; neither page says which keys have which type check beyond the SDK's `Settings` types, nor the wording for them |

| `src/settings/general/session-overrides.ts` | A `--model` flag or a `model` key outside `availableModels` is skipped for the next source; an entry allows a model when it is the start of its name or of one of its dash-separated parts (`opus` allows `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-opus-5` allows `claude-opus-5-5`), `[]` allows none, and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` is not gated | the SDK describes the entries (family aliases, version prefixes, full IDs) and model-config.md says the list constrains `/model`, `--model` and the `model` key; neither gives the matching algorithm or says what the variable does |

| `src/host/session-environment.ts` | Environment-file coordination is cached per session core while settings env is resolved on each use; host.update never changes effect ports. | The docs define process/session lifetimes, not a host update API. |
| `src/host/session-folders.ts`, `src/host/session-agents.ts` | Updated source locks immediately filter already scanned folders; sources omitted by a locked scan need a reload after unlocking. | Reusing a previous scan while applying live policy is the host's cache choice. |
| `src/mods/load/compile-output.ts`, `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | `ModCompiler` returns `scan` (an ES module) and `run` (code and a format word the kit never reads) apart; a result with only a `run` of format `esm` serves both; a `scan` output that is CommonJS (no import or export, and a `require`, `module` or `exports`) is refused with `the scan output is CommonJS, not an ES module; ...`; code the parser cannot read is left to the scan's own message. Input: scan `exports.register = function register(on) {}` | The docs say a mod is written as an ES module and that the scan reads it; they do not say the engine must run ESM, and a host with an isolate without module support runs a bundle in another format |
| `src/mods/load/loader.ts`, `src/mods/load/engine-faults.ts` | A compiler or a runtime that throws instead of returning issues is a `hooks module did not load` line, never a throw out of `load`; a stack overflow is `<file>: the engine ran out of stack loading the file (code nested too deep): <message>`, any other throw is its message under the file name; `engineStackIssue('link', ...)` is the documented linking reason for a runtime to return | troubleshoot.md gives the reasons, not who raises them; the kit never evaluates a module, so the host's runtime does, and the kit owns the words |
| `src/mods/load/scan/parse.ts` | The message in `code nested too deep to scan (the parser stopped: <message>)` is the parser's message without the `(line:col)` it appends | The page says the reason "gives no line number" |
| `src/mods/chain/matcher.ts`, `src/mods/chain/invoke.ts`, `src/mods/ports.ts` | With a `MatcherEngine` a regular expression is compiled and tested there (`compile(source, flags)` is synchronous and throws a `SyntaxError` for an invalid one; `test(value, { ms, signal })` may answer later); the flags `g` and `y` are dropped as for the in-thread test; plain values and lists never reach the engine; the engine has 100 ms (`MATCHER_MS`, `matcherMs`) per test and its signal aborts at that point; an alternative whose test was refused or timed out counts as no match and another allowed value may still match; a hook whose matcher had no match and a fault is skipped and reported `<mod>: <event> hook skipped: the matcher for "<field>" ran over its <ms> ms limit` (kind `timeout`) or `... was refused by the engine: <message>` (kind `throw`), and the chain goes on to the next hook; with no engine the pattern runs in this thread as before | events.md says a field can be a regular expression and says nothing of where it runs or of a test that does not end; a pattern from a mod's author can hold the host thread for ever |
| `src/mods/load/skip-reports.ts`, `src/mods/types.ts` | `HookReport.kind` is `throw`, `timeout` or `wrong-shape` (a result refused as nested too deep counts as `wrong-shape`; a skip for re-entry is not reported); the host's `report` receives the first line of each mod, event and kind and the debug log receives every one; a mod registered again or unregistered starts over | troubleshoot.md says the line appears once for each event and kind "until the mod reloads" and that the debug log has every occurrence, and names three causes without calling them kinds |
| `src/mods/chain/dispatch.ts`, `src/mods/types.ts` | `DispatchOptions.before(e)` runs ahead of the first mod's hook; a result other than `undefined` is the event's answer and no hook or Claude Code behaviour runs. The host puts managed `PreToolUse` hooks there and the other settings hooks in `core` | events.md fixes where each kind of `PreToolUse` hook runs and leaves how a host expresses it open |
| `src/mods/ui/elements/client.ts`, `src/mods/ui/runtime.ts` | An empty or blank `Client` error message is the reason `the module failed without a message`, in the line and in `ui.fault`'s `e.reason` | The page names the reason for an error "with no message", not whether blank text is one |
| `src/mods/load/scan/parse.ts`, `src/mods/load/engine-faults.ts` | An error is a stack overflow when it is a `RangeError` or its message says `maximum call stack size exceeded`, `too much recursion`, `stack overflow` or `not enough stack space`; an ordinary error that only mentions a stack (`stack is empty`) is its message under the file name | The page names the two reasons for running out of stack, not how a host's engine says it; the words are those of V8, SpiderMonkey and JavaScriptCore |
| `src/mods/load/engine-faults.ts` | The `the engine ran out of stack linking the file and its imports` reason is the words only (`engineStackIssue('link', ...)`): nothing in the kit detects linking, a host's runtime returns the issue; it is tested outside `spec(...)` and its id is not proven | The kit never evaluates a module, so it cannot tell linking from loading |
| `src/mods/load/commands/validate.ts` | `validate` reports the scan problem of a compiler output (CommonJS scan, no ES module to read) even when the compiler also listed issues, and the compiler's first issue only when it gave no output | Judgment row 1 of this stream: validate fails with the same message as a load, and a load reports the scan problem |
| `src/mods/load/scan/rules.ts`, `src/mods/load/scan/file-rules.ts` | The reasons for `$` passed to a method, an inner or imported function (`$ is passed to <name>, which the scan cannot read: ...`), a destructured `$` (`$ is used as a value`), `$` indexed with a computed name and an import of a bare specifier or one outside the plugin directory are the kit's words, not the official CLI's (`claude plugin validate` 2.1.319 words them differently); `check-reads-wording.test.ts` asserts them outside `spec(...)` | create.md says each of these "fails validation" and quotes a message only for `$.ui is used as a value`; the kit's wording has not been matched to the oracle |
| `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | A `hooks.json` with no `modules` key (or the key misspelled) loads no module and says nothing, so a session shows no line | troubleshoot.md says only that `validate` lists no `hooks` line for it |
| `src/mods/load/mod-set.ts`, `src/mods/load/host.ts` | A mod is keyed by its name: `load` of a plugin whose name is loaded unloads the old copy first (so the failure line gets the `; the version loaded before is unloaded` suffix) and never adds a second copy; `unloadMod` also takes the plugin out of what `reload` loads, answers whether it unloaded a mod, and never unloads the built-in guard | The page says to disable or uninstall a plugin to turn one mod off and names a single mod per name (loading.md, a name is held by one plugin); it does not say what a second `load` or a repeated off does |
| `src/mods/load/mod-set.ts`, `src/mods/load/order.ts` | A mod loaded by `loadMod` after others runs last in its tier, because the chain runs a tier in the order its mods registered; its tier and whether it may load come from the policy over the whole loaded set (`orderOne`); when `prependPlugins`, `appendPlugins` or the dependencies would have put it before a mod already loaded, a debug line says so (`<list> lists <id> before <id>, which is already loaded: <name> runs after it`); `loadMod` loads the built-in guard first when policy needs it, so the first mod is refused like any other | events.md gives the order for a full set; a mod added later cannot move the mods before it without loading them again, which would run their top-level code twice |
| `src/mods/load/guard.ts`, `src/mods/load/loader.ts`, `src/mods/load/wiring.ts` | A mod's `unload` runs once however often it is called, and takes the mod out of the approval ledger and the refusal book | The docs describe no second unload; a second `chain.unregister` could remove a mod loaded again under the same name |
| `src/mods/load/where-mods-run.ts`, `src/mods/load/policy.ts` | Wording of the refusals that name the place (`plugins are not available in WSL sessions of the Desktop app`, `the plugin does not reach the cloud session`); an unknown VS Code extension version draws nothing; a cloud plugin reaches the session only when the host says so (`reachesCloudSession: true`); built-in mods are not refused by the place | overview.md's table says "No" and "Yes, for a plugin that reaches the cloud session" without a message, and names no default for a missing version or flag |
| `src/mods/load/where-mods-run.ts` | `modsVersionProblem` reads `mods need Claude Code v<minimum> or later; this is v<version>`, or `this is an unreadable version (<text>)` for a version that is no dotted number, which is too old | Overview.md names the versions (terminal 2.1.287, Desktop 2.1.286) and no message |
| `src/mods/load/line-channel.ts`, `src/mods/load/mod-set.ts` | The line for a person is `<mod>: hooks module not loaded: <reason>` (the debug log keeps `hooks module <id> not loaded: <reason>`); a `claude -p` run in an output format other than text, or without `--plugin-dir`, keeps the line in the debug log; an interactive session with neither a plugin directory nor hot reloading has the debug log only; hot reloading is read from the host on every line (`ModsHostOptions.hotReload`, a getter), so the answer to the question moves later lines to the transcript | troubleshoot.md says the line "starts with the mod's name" and names stderr for `claude -p --plugin-dir` in the default text format, and the transcript for a session that hot-reloads a plugin directory |
| `src/mods/load/written-mods.ts` | `writtenModRefusal` checks "nobody to approve", then the trust prompt, then the settings, in that order, and its wording is ours; `dontAsk` mode is the one permission mode that cannot ask; managed `allowManagedHooksOnly` and the guard's `allowManagedModsOnly` also keep a mod Claude wrote from loading, beside the keys the page names (`disableSideloadFlags`, `disableAllHooks`, `--safe-mode`, `--bare`) | create.md lists three kinds of session and, for the last, names the flags and says managed settings "block it" |
| `src/mods/load/written-mods.ts` | `Not now` ends the hot-reloading question for the session; unanswered questions add up across answers picked in between; the state a host keeps is `{ enabled, unanswered, declined }` | create.md and troubleshoot.md say a question that ends three times unanswered leaves hot reloading off and that a dismissed one does not count; they do not say when `Not now` asks again |
| `src/mods/load/change-notice.ts`, `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | The line is `<mod>: ` followed by the page's sentence and the reason with one full stop; the reason is the loader's own (`hooks module did not load: <file>: ...`); `earlierRuns` is false only when the failed reload also failed to put the previous version back; the line is plain text, and keeping it out of the model's context ("Claude doesn't see this line") is the host's, as the kit does not own the transcript | The page gives the two sentences and says the line "starts with the mod's name and ends with the reason" |
| `src/mods/load/health.ts`, `src/mods/load/crash-recovery.ts` | The count of untraceable stops is per session with no time window and no pause before a restart; a stop traced to a loaded mod does not count toward the three; `reload()` starts the count over; the traced mod stays among the plugins the host holds, so `/reload-plugins` loads it again (the hook may be fixed); a stop traced to a name that is not a loaded mod, or to a built-in one, is untraced | troubleshoot.md gives the three stops and the line, no window or backoff (Localix's 60 s window and backoff are its own, not the docs'), and says `/reload-plugins` loads the mods again and 'Fix the hook' for the traced one |
| `src/mods/load/crash-recovery.ts`, `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | A module in a stopped worker may not answer its disposal: the mod is unloaded and the error ignored (an unanswered disposal is not simulated by the in-process runtime); a fresh copy that does not load puts its `hooks module did not load` line in the debug log and `onNotice`, and that mod stays off; the fresh copy is made from what the mod was loaded from, with no new read of its files | the page says Claude Code loads the module again after the worker was replaced, nothing about a dead worker's disposal or a copy that fails |
| `src/mods/api/replay.ts`, `src/mods/load/host-support.ts` | A call is told from another across copies by the mod, the kind of call and its ordinal among the calls of that kind made while that mod's `session.start` dispatch was open; the repeated call is answered from the ledger around the whole call, so a mod before the caller sees the first call only and its `prompt.submit`, `command.run` or `agent.spawn` hook does not fire again in the fresh copy; a failed first call is not recorded, so the fresh copy makes it; a mod that is unloaded (`unloadMod`, a stop traced to it, the third untraced stop) is forgotten by the ledger; `/reload-plugins`, `loadMod` and `reloadMod` start the ledger over (their `session.start` makes its calls), only a worker replacement repeats; the line is said once for each kind of call in a fresh copy | the page says only what the mod sees (the call resolves with the result of its first run and the line) and that the cause is the module being loaded again, 'for example after the hooks worker crashed and was replaced' |
| `src/mods/load/line-channel.ts` | The line of a worker stop reaches the transcript of an interactive session (the page says so for the line of three stops; the line of one mod unloaded has the same place); a `claude -p` run reads it where it reads a module that did not load; the line of a call not made again is read where a skipped hook is | the page names the place for the three-stops line only |
| `src/mods/api/call.ts` | `$.command.register` and `$.tool.register` from a module that is no longer loaded are refused with the same line as `$.agent.register` (the page names only `$.agent.register`); the line has the mod's name first and ends `(it was reloaded or removed)` | a registration from an old copy would add what no copy owns, whatever the namespace |
| `src/mods/api/gate.ts`, `src/mods/load/host.ts` | The turns that count toward `--max-turns`, those of the mod's prompts and of the subagents it spawns, are the session's own count (`ModSession.turns()`): the host counts a subagent's turn, the kit reads the total; `input.setLimits(limits)` replaces both limits at once for the next call (a limit left out is no limit), for a host that outlives a run and rebuilds its settings for each one; the turn count itself starts over at `userInput()` only | api.md names the limits and says what counts, not how a host that serves many runs changes them |
| `src/mods/api/gate.ts` | When several reasons apply the reported one is: input ended, over budget, at the turn limit, then stopped; the turn limit counts the turns since the last input of the user (the session's own count at `userInput()`, 0 at the start); `usage().cost` is read as a number of US dollars, and a value that is not a number is no spend; the spend never resets | the page lists the four reasons and says which ones a next input lifts (stopped, the turn count) and which it does not (budget, ended input), not their order or the shape of `cost` |
| `src/mods/api/refusal.ts` | A refused call is judged after the hooks of the mods before it, where the host's effect would run; a refused `$.command.run` rejects with `<mod>: $.command.run: <reason>`; `$.tool.call` refuses Bash with `run_in_background: true` (flat among the arguments or in `input`), Monitor and CronCreate and no other call; `$.session.send` is not refused by the kit: it gives the host's port the reason the session refuses new work now (`send(to, text, signal, refusal?)`, read after the hooks of the mods before), and the port, which knows whether the target is a subagent that finished, a subagent that runs or a teammate, answers `{ isDelivered: false, reason }` worded with `unsentReason.subagent(id, reason)` (`Agent "<id>" was not resumed: <reason>.`) or `unsentReason.teammate(name, reason)` (`Teammate "<name>" was not messaged: <reason>.`) | the table gives the results, not where in the call they are decided or how an argument names background work; the roster's row shape is not documented |
| `src/mods/api/tool.ts`, `src/mods/api/ports.ts` | `ModTools.call` is given `held()`, which answers whether the session's input is held (a Stop, a limit, the end of input): a foreground Bash or PowerShell command that reaches its timeout while it is held is ended by the host, which resolves with `isError` and the line `HELD_AT_TIMEOUT` in the output; the kit gives the question and the line, and the ending is the host's, as it owns the process and its timeout; the result shape is the tool's own, so the kit does not build it. The same for the other rows of "Work your mod started before the interruption" the kit has no part in: the events a monitor raises while input is held (dropped, except the notice that the monitor ended), the finish notice of a subagent resumed with `$.session.send` (it waits), and a schedule coming due (it runs after the input) belong to the host's monitors, subagents and scheduler, which the kit does not model | api.md gives what the user sees; a process, a monitor and a notice queue are effects the kit does not own (L3), and the result of a foreground tool is the host's shape |
| `src/mods/api/gate.ts`, `src/mods/api/refusal.ts`, `src/mods/api/replay.ts` | The kit models the latest Claude Code release only: the interruption handling of calls other than `$.prompt.submit` and `$.command.run` (api.md says v2.1.304), the refusal of a prompt at the turn limit (v2.1.299), subagent turns counting toward `--max-turns` (v2.1.304) and the dedupe of a fresh copy's calls (earlier than v2.1.292 the replay did not dedupe) are applied whatever version the host reports; a host that serves an earlier release does not call `interrupt` / `limits` or leaves the ledger out | the page names a minimum release for each; the kit has no version of its own to compare with, the host does (`versionAtLeast`) |
| `src/mods/load/mod-set.ts` | Every change to the set (`load`, `loadMod`, `unloadMod`, `reload`, `reloadMod`, `crashed`, `unloadAll`) runs after the one before it ends, so a stop of the worker during `reload()` is handled when the reload is done (the reloaded mods get their fresh copy) and two stops in a row never rewrite the loaded mods at once; a runtime's call that loads a module must settle when its worker stops, since a stop waits for it | troubleshoot.md describes one stop, not stops that overlap another change |
| `src/mods/load/loader.ts` | A fresh copy of a mod the loader has no record of (never reached by a mod it loaded) says `<mod>: hooks module did not load: the module it was loaded from is not known` and stays off, never silently | the page does not cover a copy that cannot be made |
| `src/mods/api/ports.ts`, `src/mods/api/refusal.ts` | `ModPrompts.submit` and `ModCommands.run` take a second `queue` signal that aborts when the user stops the turn before the call ran, and the kit drops the call itself at that moment; a call that already ran is never dropped | the page says a prompt or command 'still in the queue' is dropped; the queue is the host's, so the kit asks it to let go |
| `src/mods/api/describe.ts` | `describeApi(api)` describes `$` as plain data: each namespace in the reference's order, each member with `kind` (`value`, `call`, `sync`, `timer`, `stream`), `async` and, for a call of the reference's table, the event it fires; it reads the live `$` (a namespace another mod added on `engine.create` is described with no events, a withheld one is absent) and reports no arity | the docs publish the declarations of the mods API through the build's types, not a function that describes them, and the kit reads only the pages; a host that runs mods in another realm needs the shape as data to rebuild `$` there |
| `src/mods/api/state.ts`, `src/mods/load/host.ts` | `ModsHost.resetState()` resets every `$.state` value (what the host calls for `/clear`, `/resume` and `/branch`) and does not fire `classic.SessionStart` itself, which is the settings hooks' event; each value that was held counts as written, so a site that read it is drawn again and shows the default | the page says the commands reset every value to its default and that the drawing then shows the default, not which event redraws it; the `ui.render` hook that writes a value it read is not refused (the page says it "can't write it" and names no result), only its own write does not redraw it |
| `src/mods/api/store.ts` | A `set` or `delete` of one plugin's store waits for the one before it on the same `ModStore` port, so two sessions of this process that write different keys at the same time both stay; a `get` then a `set` by a mod stays non-atomic, and a host that shares the store file between processes guards it with a lock of its own | the page promises "a `set` changes only its own key" and "a `get` followed by a `set` isn't atomic", and the port reads and writes the whole store |
| `src/mods/api/gate.ts` | `RunGate.notice(kind, deliver)` is how a host raises a notice for the conversation: while the session is stopped a subagent's finish notice (`agent.finished`) and the notice that a monitor ended (`monitor.ended`) wait and are delivered in order when the user's next input arrives, an event a monitor raises (`monitor.event`) is dropped, and with no interruption each is delivered at once; the monitor and the subagent are the host's, the kit holds the delivery | the page says what happens to each notice and leaves the machinery to the host, as it does for the queue of prompts and commands |
| `src/mods/events/reasons.ts` | `withheldResultText`: the note `<tool> ran, and a plugin withheld its result:` precedes the reason only when the tool ran and succeeded; a call refused before it ran, or one that failed, gives Claude the reason alone. `promptDroppedText` is `Prompt dropped by a hook: <reason>` | events.md quotes the note for a tool that ran and succeeded and says nothing of the other two; the prompt line is quoted word for word |
| `src/mods/events/read-only.ts` | `toolReadOnlyReport` answers `true` for Read, Grep, Glob, WebSearch, WebFetch and Agent, for a Bash command the host's read-only check passes and for an MCP tool whose `readOnlyHint` is `true`; every other tool (Edit, Write, PowerShell, a tool the docs do not list) and an MCP tool with no hint leaves the field absent | The page names "some tools, such as" four and WebFetch and Agent as examples; the rest is read as "the tool does not report it", since the value is the tool's own report and absent is the default |
| `src/mods/events/input-review.ts` | `inputChangedDenial` denies a changed input in auto mode only, with the page's reason followed by `; issue the call once more exactly as recorded`; the two reasons (`after the model wrote it`, `after auto mode reviewed it`) are chosen by the host's `review` | troubleshoot.md says the message tells Claude to issue the call once more as recorded and quotes the reason, not the instruction's words; which of the two reasons a session gets depends on the connection, which the host knows |
| `src/mods/events/command.ts`, `src/mods/load/host.ts` | `ModsHost.runCommand` answers a command a mod registered that reaches the end of the `command.run` chain with `<mod> registered /<command> but no command.run hook answered it. Add a command.run hook for /<command> that returns { text }.`; any other command ends in the host's `core` | troubleshoot.md quotes the first half of the reply and says it "tells you to add a hook" |
| `src/mods/api/settings.ts` | A `$.settings.read({ key })` whose hook answered `{ value }` with a value that is not an object (an array, a string) resolves to `undefined` for the key | events.md says to "return a whole object and not an array" because the key is taken from the value, and gives no result for a value a key cannot be taken from |
| `src/mods/api/settings-layers.ts` | `settingsLayersForMods` maps the settings stream's sources to the six names (`managed` is `policy`) and leaves out the settings an embedding host passes (`parent`) and a `--add-dir` directory's (`addDir`), which the six names do not include; a source with no settings is not listed | events.md lists `plugin`, `user`, `project`, `local`, `flag` and `policy` and says one entry for each source in use that holds settings |
| `src/mods/api/rules.ts` | A `settings.read` hook's answer must be an object with `{ value }` or a string `{ deny }`; anything else fails the hook like a throw | reference.md gives `next(e)`, `{ value }` or `{ deny: reason }`; a wrong answer is a failure everywhere else the table lists an answer |
| `src/mods/api/gate.ts`, `src/mods/load/wiring.ts` | The toast `Stopped: type a message to continue; a mod cannot resume work after Stop` is raised once for each refused call of the kit's own refusals (`$.prompt.submit`, `$.command.run`, `$.agent.spawn`, a background `$.tool.call`) while the session is stopped, owned by `/engine` on the toast board (a valid mod name cannot collide); a `$.session.send` the host refuses and the other reasons (turn limit, budget, ended input) raise none | troubleshoot.md says a mod tried to start work and Claude Code did not let it, without a list of the calls or an owner of the toast |
| `src/mods/load/guard.ts`, `src/mods/load/line-channel.ts` | `tried to lift a deny rule in your settings` goes to the debug log and, as a notice, to the transcript of an interactive session (the debug log only in `claude -p`), once for each mod in a session; the other two messages of the guard table are quoted as the page prints them, without a fourth part naming the option | The table says "once for each mod in a session" for the row and "names the option" of each message, while it quotes only the part the message contains; the kit adds no words it was not given |
| `src/mods/events/turn.ts` | `turn.step` carries `turnId` (optional) beside `agentId`, `model` and `effort` | events.md says `e.turnId` of `turn.start` identifies the turn in the other two events |
| `src/testing/mods/claude.ts` | The test's `$.settings.read(input)` fires `settings.read` with `layers: []` unless the test gives `layers` | events.md says `e.layers` is "Empty in `claude plugin test`" |

| `src/mods/api/settings-layers.ts` | Plugin settings form a single lowest-precedence layer by a shallow last-plugin-wins merge. | The docs name one plugin layer and do not prescribe a nested merge algorithm. |
| `src/mods/load/wiring.ts` | The engine's stopped-work toast belongs to `/engine`, an owner no mod name can equal. | The docs quote its text but no internal UI ownership key. |

| `src/mods/load/tool-flow.ts` | An unresolved ask that remains unresolved after the host callback is refused as `Permission was not granted` when it has no reason. The tool's `isError: true` marks failure for withheld-result presentation. | The docs require a permission decision and a success-only prefix, but do not prescribe the embedding host's fallback wording or execution port. |

| `src/cli/tag-plan.ts` | The host supplies the directory after argument defaulting, full validation findings, git state and the matching ancestor marketplace entry; the plan uses argument vectors, not a shell, and quotes only its human preview. Refusal wording and preview labels not quoted in the docs are judgments. | The release-tag docs prescribe the checks and successful messages, not a port API, fallback failures, or preview layout. |
| `src/cli/configure-dialog.ts` | Explicit configure with zero declared options opens no empty dialog. Sensitive starting values are never returned to the UI adapter. | The docs define when options are requested, not an empty dialog or secret-prefill API. |
| `src/loading/resolve/monitor-trigger.ts` | Omitting the release uses the tracked release's capabilities; an unparseable supplied release with no feature flags fails the minimum-version check. The host reports whether feature-flag fetching is available. | The docs give a minimum release and affected configurations, not the embedding-host interface. |
| `src/settings/host-switches.ts` | `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_GIT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SYNC_PLUGIN_INSTALL_TIMEOUT_MS` take plain digits above zero; anything else leaves the default (120000) or no bound. `DO_NOT_TRACK` reads `1` and `true` as on. | env-vars.md names the defaults, not the invalid values; it calls `DO_NOT_TRACK` "a standard boolean" without listing spellings. |
| `src/settings/channels/contract.ts` | A channel tag escapes `&`, `"`, `<`, `>` in attribute values and ignores a `source` key in `meta`; the permission capability counts only as an object. | channels-reference.md shows the tag and the key rule, not escaping or a `source` clash. |
| `src/settings/channels/sanitize.ts`, `relay.ts` | Each neutralized character (direction override, invisible, quote and angle-bracket lookalike) becomes U+FFFD; 3,500 code points are kept as 1,750 at each end around `⋯ N code points elided ⋯`; an array over 10,000 items is "extremely large"; credential masking covers the common provider prefixes and skips a token touching a path or URL character; a repeated request ID is never issued again as valid (its verdicts are dropped). | "Permission request fields" names the effects, not the character sets, the split of the limit, the size threshold or the token list. |
| `src/settings/marketplaces/refusal-messages.ts` | The three refusal lines (skills-directory plugins blocked, command-sourced plugins disabled, plugin blocked) carry the documented first line and the facts the docs list; the wording of the remaining sentences is the kit's. | plugins/troubleshooting.md quotes each entry's first line and describes the rest. |
| `src/agents/prompt-surface.ts` | A foreground subagent's prompt does not name the subagent; a background one does and Esc denies one call. | tools-reference.md says foreground prompts match the main conversation's and names the subagent only for background ones. |
| `src/rules/tools/shell-selection.ts` | An interactive `!` command uses PowerShell only when `defaultShell` is `powershell` and the PowerShell tool is enabled; every other case is Bash. | tools-reference.md says `defaultShell: powershell` "requires the PowerShell tool", not what happens without it. |
| `src/settings/managed/approval.ts` | Approval-needing settings are exactly those the docs name: the three shell-command keys, the three sandbox binaries, the listed sandbox network and isolation keys, `sandbox.credentials` unless every `files`/`envVars` rule is `deny`, any hook, and the env variables of the docs' lists. The proxy and base-URL name patterns, the truthy/falsy spellings (`1 true yes on` / `0 false no off`) and the header-name word list are the kit's; other env variables apply without the dialog. | server-managed-settings.md says "such as" for most lists and gives no complete variable list. |
| `src/format/host-errors.ts` | Where errors.md quotes only the parts of a message (the `Remote managed settings failed to load (<cause>)` lead and its `using cached policy` / `no remote policy applied` ending, the `have now loaded` line and what follows it) the kit joins them with `: `; the plural of `skill or command name refused` is `names`; the entries of `Sandbox allow entries are ignored` read `entry` for a count of one. | errors.md quotes one example per message and says "the rest of the line says" without giving the punctuation or the plural forms. |
| `src/rules/bash/wildcard-warning.ts` | The source in parentheses is the documented location of the rule's settings file (`~/.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, the `--settings` file) or `--allowed-tools`; the git sentence is added for a `git` rule only; a managed or session source is `managed settings` or `session`. | errors.md gives one example for a project file and says the source is "a settings file path, or the `--allowed-tools` flag itself"; it quotes the git sentence for a git rule only. |
| `src/loading/workspace-trust.ts` | `untrustedWorkspaceWarning` says `entry` for a count of one. | errors.md quotes the plural form only. |
| `src/mods/ui/keyboard/keys.ts` | Tab wraps from the last control to the first; Up and Down stop at the first and last control; Enter on no control and a page key on a drawing that fits do nothing; a key the drawing does not take is a key for the prompt. Ctrl+X then X is read in either case. | interface.md's "What each key does" names each key's effect, not the ends of the list or an unbound key. |
| `src/mods/ui/keyboard/focus.ts` | The Desktop app grants `focus: true` by the terminal's rule; a click focuses a pane in every app except the terminal without fullscreen rendering; `autoFocus` picks the first control that sets it. | The docs state the rule for the terminal and the VS Code panel, not the Desktop app, and name no tie for two `autoFocus` controls. |
| `src/mods/ui/keyboard/vscode.ts` | A status line is cut at two rows first, then at 200 characters; `$.ui.status` and `$.ui.toast` apply the cuts only when the interface port says the app is `vscode`. | "at 200 characters or two rows" does not say which cut comes first. |
| `src/mods/api/fetch-policy.ts` | The verdict is a function of facts the host supplies (organization policy on WebFetch, whether the mod is built in, whether the request carries the session credentials); the kit does not see the request, so the host tells it whether credentials ride on it. | admin.md names the cases, not the interface. |
| `src/marketplace/seed-registry.ts` | `applySeeds` takes the seeds in path-list order and the first one that declares a name wins; a seed entry replaces a user entry whole and its `autoUpdate` is `false`. | org.md gives the rules in prose and no registry interface. |

| `src/marketplace/plugin-hints.ts` | A tag with surrounding whitespace is still on its own line; an unquoted value that is followed by a stray word is no tag attributes list (line stays in the output); the order in which failing prompt checks are reported. | cli-hints.md lists the checks, not the order or malformed forms. |
| `src/marketplace/name-conflicts.ts` | Wording of the `/plugin` rows for a skills-directory copy shadowed by an installed plugin or another skills directory, for a synced copy, and the tail of the hooks note after `Its hooks.json hooks do not run`. | loading.md quotes only the start or the session-only row. |

| `src/loading/resolve/hook-command.ts`, `src/hooks/handlers/command/placeholders.ts` | `HookCommandOptions.shellPaths` and `HookEngineOptions.shellPaths: 'environment-reference'` leaves a bash shell-form hook's path variables as `${NAME}` for the shell to read from the process environment (which `environment` fills); the default `pasted` writes the paths in | The docs say the placeholders are substituted and that the variables are also exported; they do not address a path that holds shell metacharacters |
| `package.json`, `tests/protocol/package-types.test.ts` | Every SDK imported by the protocol boundary is a package dependency or peer, not only a development dependency; the MCP SDK is a peer alongside the Agent SDK. | Public declarations retain these official type imports. Consumers must be able to resolve them independently of this repository's development install; sharing the host's SDK avoids another runtime copy. This packaging choice is not Claude Code compatibility. |
| `package.json`, `tests/protocol/package-types.test.ts` | The `./layout` entry points directly at the standard layout registry, sharing the root export's identity. | Small classifiers should not pull all engine modules into a bundle or its cache inputs. | `src/rules/decide/file-targets.ts`, `tests/rules/bash-file-targets.test.ts` | `bashFileTargets` recognizes the file commands `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `less`, `more`, `bat`, `nl`, `wc`, `sort`, `uniq`, `diff`, `cmp`, `xxd`, `od`, `hexdump`, `strings`, `file`, `stat`, `md5sum`, `shasum`, `sha256sum`, `base64`, `source`, `.`, `jq`, `yq`, `awk`, `cut`, `tr`, `column`, `grep`, `rg` (read), `touch`, `rm`, `truncate`, `chmod`, `chown`, `mkdir`, `rmdir` (write), `cp`, `mv`, `install`, `ln`, `rsync` (last operand written) and `sed` (written with `-i`); the first operand of `grep`, `jq`, `awk`, `sed` and the like is a pattern, not a file; every word that is not an option is an operand (so `head -n 3 f` also names `3`); a `cd` earlier in the line does not move later operands | permissions.md names `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed` and `tee` "such as" and gives no list; a lexical reading errs toward naming more files, which only makes a deny rule stricter |
| `src/marketplace/index.ts`, `tests/marketplace/ranges-overlap.test.ts` | `rangesOverlap` is part of the public API. | Hosts that fetch a dependency at the combined range of several plugins need the question `combineRanges` asks before the fetch; the docs describe the conflict, not a host's fetch order. |
| `src/skills/scan/read.ts`, `scan-types.ts`, `head.ts`, `container.ts`, `src/foundation/folder-read.ts`, `src/agents/catalog/scan.ts`, `src/host/session-skills.ts`, `tests/skills/scan/lean.test.ts` | metadata-first catalog: a scan entry holds the skill or command without its body and a `readBody(signal)` that opens the folder again through the port at each call (nothing memoized; `undefined` when the file is gone or no longer loads, the port's rejection otherwise); a port that rejects a read at scan time makes that file a warning and the scan goes on, an aborted signal still rejects; a session expands a folder skill with the body read at that moment. | skills.md, "Edit a skill during a session" says an edit is picked up within the session; it does not say how a host keeps memory small for a large catalog, nor what a refused read is. |
